If you are unaware of the state to which the controversies regarding the **ANTICHRIST** <note>The author refers to the long-standing theological debates regarding the identity of the Antichrist, a figure traditionally associated with the end times in Christian eschatology.</note> and the interpretation of the number 666 <note>original: "numeri 666" — This refers to the "Number of the Beast" from the Book of Revelation 13:18, a central puzzle in biblical scholarship.</note> have been reduced, and if you are not equipped with that skill which is necessary to understand these things: I foresee that all these things written here will seem to you to be nothing but a tangled labyrinth of curious and useless speculations. There are, therefore, two things I ask of you: namely, that you either stir up your own diligence to attain the skill necessary for understanding these matters, or that you exercise your own sobriety <note>Here, "sobriety" refers to intellectual humility or restraint—knowing the limits of one's own understanding.</note> by leaving those things which surpass your grasp to the more learned, and to those at least more skilled in these affairs. These men, perhaps for the very reason that such things surpass your intellect, will judge it all the more likely that this is the true Interpretation. In any case, you should rightly realize this: it is in no man's power, much less mine, to make straight those things which God has made crooked; <note>A reference to Ecclesiastes 7:13: "Consider the work of God: for who can make that straight, which he hath made crooked?"</note> or to render plain and easy for everyone to understand those things which,

# TO THE READER.

But if you are one of those readers of whom (a) *Lucilius* speaks—one who will understand more from what I have written than I, the author, understood myself—then my request to you is not what he asked (that you should refrain from reading). Instead, the wiser, the more learned, and the more keen-sighted you are, the more earnestly I urge you to deign to examine these pages. I offer them to you with confidence, yet submissively, out of reverence for the judgment of those more sound than myself.

I am not unaware that it is human nature for everyone to be partial to their own thoughts and discoveries, and for many to be utterly enamored with them. I acknowledge also that the human heart is "deceitful above all things" <note>A reference to Jeremiah 17:9: "The heart is deceitful above all things, and desperately wicked."</note> and prone to error; indeed, it practices this exceptional deception above all others: it cheats even itself. Finally, I recognize that even one who knows all these pitfalls can still "hold a lie in his right hand." <note>A reference to Isaiah 44:20, describing the self-delusion of one who cannot see the truth even when it is right in front of them.</note> Yet I trust that my confidence in the truth of this **Interpretation** arises from the very evidence of truth and reason, and not from the blindness of my own mind.

### TO THE READER.

I offer these things; but only after mature, or at least long, deliberation. Indeed, many years have already passed since I publicly presented and defended certain chief chapters of these matters in the **Theological Schools** <gloss>at Oxford <note>original: "Oxoniæ"</note></gloss> <margin>In the year 1625.</margin>; yet neither at that time, nor at any other since then, have I been able to hear or learn any weighty reasons that could cause me to doubt either their probability or their truth. <note>The Theological Schools at Oxford were the prestigious setting for formal academic disputations, suggesting the author's ideas have already withstood rigorous scholarly scrutiny.</note>

Nevertheless, if anyone should be able to refute or overturn—either by the authority of the **Holy Scriptures** or by weighty reasons—the possibility or probability of this **Interpretation**, or of any part of it that touches upon the heart and substance of the matter, I myself shall be ready and willing to demolish with my own hands what would then be the empty fabrications of my imagination. I would even rejoice to see their ruins and rubble serve a purpose, by leveling the ground or laying a foundation upon which a firmer building might be constructed. But I promise better things for myself regarding this **Interpretation** (even if I speak thus), and things which are joined to the revelation of truth, which shall not be abolished.

Nor would I wish for anyone to think it less likely to be true simply because it comes from me, a man who is prominent neither in the **Church** nor in the **State** <note>original: "Republica" — referring to the public sphere or the Commonwealth.</note> (unless perhaps for my infirmities). <note>The author employs a common rhetorical trope of humility, suggesting that the truth of a work should be judged on its own merits rather than the social status or health of its creator.</note> Great

(a) A certain recent Anonymous Writer, to this effect, helpfully notes the following: *<unclear>But</unclear> if this is so, it is not permitted even to the greatest minds to arrogantly claim the discovery of this <term>number<note>This refers to the "number of the beast" (666) from the biblical Book of Revelation, a central mystery for scholars of this era.</note></term> for themselves; nor can it be denied that God can reveal the logic of this number to one of very small intellect. Nor let it seem improbable to anyone that the true meaning and interpretation of this number—which remained undiscovered for many centuries—has finally been perceived and revealed in these times. Indeed, these happy times—in which Christian Princes and Commonwealths are powerful and ready to openly resist the **Pope** and profess the truth in public—are most suitable (as (b) Foxe <note>John Foxe (1516–1587), an English historian best known for his "Book of Martyrs," who also wrote extensively on the Book of Revelation.</note> notes) for the revealing of this mystery. # TO THE READER.

...follows; for the matter does not turn on this one piece of evidence alone. Many other signs are open and approved, yet this one remains hidden and obscure. And although it is probable that it should be revealed before the end of the Antichrist, we nevertheless see the Beast, the Kings of the Earth, and their Armies gathered together to make war against Christ and His Army, in that final battle in which all shall be conquered and overthrown. Therefore, let everyone seek, track, and scrutinize; those who seek should not despair of discovery, for failing to recognize it is a deadly thing for those who resist the truth.

Regarding the interpretation itself: I believe this number 666 is an image and a typical representation of the whole body of the Antichrist; that is, of the entire Body of his Kingdom, State, and Hierarchy. Nearly all writers acknowledge this, and specifically *Cotterius* <note>Matthieu Cottière, a prominent 17th-century Reformed theologian known for his commentaries on the Apocalypse.</note>, who holds this as a certain foundation. He maintains that the true interpretation of this number must be explored and examined against this as a <term>touchstone<note>original: "Lydium lapidem." From these properties, the essential characteristics of numbers arise, and the mysteries—if there be any—chiefly depend, which *St. Augustine* and other writers, both sacred and secular, have observed within Numbers.

Now, concerning those things which are most essential and noteworthy in the Kingdom of the Antichrist, to which the <term>Root</term> <note>In 17th-century mathematics, the "root" (radix) usually refers to the square root of a number.</note> and the <term>Figure</term> <note>original: "Figura"; this refers to the geometric shape a number represents, such as a square or a triangle.</note> of the Number thus discovered must be applied and compared: the Holy Scriptures themselves direct us with infallible guidance and show us by example. They do this by actually applying the Root and Figure of the *opposite* Number <note>The "opposite number" refers to the numbers associated with Christ and the New Jerusalem, specifically 12 and its square, 144.</note> to those particulars which are notably opposed to them and correspond from the opposite side.

Indeed, the Root and Figure of the Number 666 are to be investigated in no other way than by calculating and computing the Number itself. And so it is expressly said in the Text: <note>original Greek: "ὁ ἔχων τὸν νοῦν, ψηφισάτω τὸν ἀριθμὸν τοῦ θηρίου"</note> *He who has understanding, let him calculate the Number of the Beast*, so that he may surely explore its Root and Figure. With the help of these, the Root and Foundation of the Antichrist’s <term>Hierarchy</term> <note>The organized body of officials or clergy that makes up the Antichrist's government.</note>, as well as his Origin, City, State, Doctrine, and many other things, may become known.

And the following Interpretation aims at this very goal; it depends chiefly upon the **Extraction of the Root** of the Number 666, just as the words of the Text directly suggest must be done to investigate the true Interpretation. And that this is the true way of computing...

# TO THE READER.

I am so confident regarding the **Number of the Beast**, that I dare now to say specifically of this interpretation (with only two words changed) what **Matthew Cotterius** (a) said generally of his own typical interpretation during the time of **King James**: *These are the matters in which I am prepared to risk the gamble of judgment. Since it is in everyone’s interest that these things be established if they do not deviate from the truth, I plead with all my prayers to learned men—and especially to Your Majesty, most Serene King—that those who are the most learned ornaments of your realm might investigate each point, note what they find, and for those things which appear certain, add (yet a further) calculation <note>original: "calculum"; the author uses a Latin term that means both a mathematical "calculation" and a "vote" or "pebble" used in casting a judgment, playing on the dual nature of his work.</note>, so that some fruit may come from it for the Lord’s Church.*

If, however, there are those who think this kind of interpretation is obscure and intricate, and relies too much on human arts and sciences: let them, I say, understand those words of the Scripture: <note>original: "Hic est sapientia, qui intellectum habet, computet Numerum Bestiæ"</note> *Here is wisdom; let him who has understanding calculate the Number of the Beast*; they will find therein enough to answer their objections abundantly. Meanwhile, I would have them weigh carefully that **St. Augustine**, **St. Jerome**, **St. Gregory**, and many others used such methods—and indeed, used them for interpretations more obscure than these—regarding various other numbers occurring elsewhere in the Holy Scriptures: and such interpretations of theirs were received with praise.

---

<margin>(a) Matthew Cotterius, in the Dedicatory Letter to the King of England before his Commentaries on the **Apocalypse** <note>The "Apocalypse" refers to the Book of Revelation.</note>.</margin>

# TO THE READER.

...themselves (a) by later Interpreters, and were held worthy to be applied to Theological truths, rather than to human arts and speculations.

I therefore acknowledge, not unwillingly, that this Interpretation is in some way difficult and obscure, especially to some; since it is easily proved that a true Interpretation ought to have been such. And just as it is obscure and intricate, so must it be confessed that it is new and unheard of in previous times. For both of these reasons, I will say of it what, in a not dissimilar case, a most celebrated Author (b) recently said of his own new Philosophy: *I know that just as it is arduous to give novelty to the old, luster to the obsolete, light to the obscure, grace to the loathed, and credit to the doubtful; so it is much more difficult to win some authority for and to establish things that are new and unheard of, against the opinions of all men.*

->But<-

***

<margin>(a) And indeed, just as we greatly praise the Doctors of the Church who, in explaining the Sacred Scriptures—in which it is believed that not a single stroke <note>original: "apex"; referring to the smallest mark in Hebrew script.</note> or iota <note>original: "jota"; the smallest letter in the Greek alphabet. This is a reference to Matthew 5:18.</note> was placed without the singular counsel and inspiration of God—pursue the secret and mystical meanings of Numbers no less piously than acutely: so we do not at all approve of those who strive to do the same in handling human disciplines, and who thrust upon us I know not what mysteries of Numbers and Magnitudes in place of the true and proper reasons and causes of natural things. *Pererius <note>Benedictus Pererius (1535–1610) was a Jesuit philosopher and theologian.</note>, Book 4 on the Ancient Philosophers, chapter 18.*</margin>

<margin>(b) William Gilbert, *On the Magnet and Magnetic Bodies* <note>William Gilbert (1544–1603) was a pioneer of electrical engineering and magnetism; this quote is from his landmark 1600 work *De Magnete*.</note>; in the Preface to the Reader.</margin>

# TO THE READER.

Regarding the **Number of the Beast**, <note>A reference to the number 666 from Revelation 13:18, a central mystery in apocalyptic studies.</note> I am so confident that I dare (with only two words swapped) to say of this specific Interpretation what Cottière (a) said generally of his own symbolic interpretation during the time of **King James**: <note>James I of England (reigned 1603–1625), who was himself a scholar of the Apocalypse.</note> *“These are the matters in which I am prepared to risk the hazard of judgments; and since it is important that these things be built up if they do not deviate from the truth, I strive with all my prayers to ask of learned men, and especially of Your Most Serene Majesty, O King, that those who are the most learned ornaments of your realm might inquire into every detail, note what they discover, and add their further approval <note>original: "calculum"; literally a "pebble" used for voting or counting, signifying a seal of approval.</note> to those things which seem certain, so that some fruit may come from this for the Church of the Lord.”*

If, however, there are those who think this kind of Interpretation is obscure and intricate, and relies too much on human arts and sciences: I say that when they understand those words of the Scripture: *“Here is wisdom; let him who has understanding calculate the Number of the Beast,”* <note>Revelation 13:18. The author argues that because the Bible commands "calculation," the use of mathematical "human arts" is divinely sanctioned.</note> they will find therein enough to answer their objections. In the meantime, I would have them consider that **Saint Augustine**, **Saint Jerome**, **Saint Gregory**, and many others used such methods—and indeed, used them on interpretations even more obscure than these—regarding various Numbers occurring elsewhere in Holy Scripture; and such interpretations of theirs were received with praise.

***

(a) Matthieu Cottière, in the Dedicatory Epistle to the King of England before the *Commentaries on the Apocalypse*. <note>Cottière was a 17th-century French Protestant theologian.</note>

<summary>The author defends his mathematical interpretation of the "Number of the Beast" by quoting Matthieu Cottière and citing the authority of Church Fathers like Augustine and Jerome. He argues that the biblical command to "calculate" justifies the use of technical or "obscure" methods in theology.</summary>

# TO THE READER.

...themselves (a) by later Interpreters, and deemed worthy to be applied to Theological truths rather than to human arts and speculations.

I therefore acknowledge, quite willingly, that this Interpretation is in some ways difficult and obscure, especially to some; for it is easily proven that a true Interpretation must necessarily be so. And just as it is obscure and intricate, so it must be admitted that it is new and unheard of in previous times. For both these reasons, I will say of it what, in a not dissimilar case, a most celebrated Author (b) recently said of his own new Philosophy: <note>The author is comparing his biblical "calculations" to the revolutionary scientific methods of the early 17th century.</note> *I know how difficult it is to give novelty to what is old, brilliance to what is obsolete, light to what is obscure, grace to what is loathed, and credibility to what is doubtful; and so it is much more difficult to win some authority for and to establish things that are new and unheard of, against all the opinions of everyone.*

->But<-

---

(a) And indeed, while we greatly praise the Doctors of the Church who, in explaining the Sacred Scriptures—wherein it is believed not a single stroke <note>original: "apex"; referring to the smallest mark in Hebrew or Greek script.</note> or iota was placed without the singular counsel and inspiration of God—pursue the secret and most mystical meanings of Numbers no less piously than sharply: so we by no means approve of those who strive to do the same in handling human disciplines, and who, instead of the true and proper reasons and causes of natural things, thrust upon us I know not what mysteries of Numbers and Magnitudes. <note>original: "Pererius, lib. 4. de Antiquis Philosophis. cap. 18." refers to Benedictus Pererius, a Jesuit scholar who argued that while numerology is valid for the Bible, it should not be used to explain the physical laws of nature.</note>

(b) Gilbert, *On the Magnet and Magnetic Bodies*; in the Preface to the Reader. <note>William Gilbert was an English physician and physicist. By quoting him, the author aligns his biblical study with the rigorous "New Science" of the 1600s.</note>

<meta>The previous page discussed how early theologians used numerical analysis to uncover divine secrets in the Bible, acknowledging that this "new" method of interpretation might seem difficult or obscure to readers. This page continues that defense by arguing that all established truths were once new and controversial.</meta>

### TO THE READER.

But all those truths which are now ancient were once new, and had their own detractors. New truths are like new friends; they must be tested first before we trust them. Therefore, I propose these matters to the wise and the learned—just as the poet Martial (a) offered himself to his friend—to be first examined and scrutinized, and only then embraced. And just as it is always the mark of a prudent person to trust an old friend, so too would it be no small imprudence to reject a new friend without any examination.

Regarding the <term>method</term> <note>original: "methodum"; referring to the systematic way of organizing the argument.</note> and the manner of composing this <term>treatise</term> <note>original: "tractatum"; a formal written work on a specific subject.</note>, I freely confess that it is not only unpolished, but even crude and rough. This is especially true in a subject like this, where the difficult material demands a more accurate form, a clear method, and refined phrasing. But I hope that my <term>logic</term> <note>original: "logicam"; the strength of the reasoning.</note> here is stronger than my <term>rhetoric</term> <note>original: "rhetorica"; the artistic style of the writing.</note>. Perhaps there will not be lacking someone who will soon clothe my thoughts in better words, and perhaps in another language, if they deserve it. For my part, I have aimed only at this: to speak in such a way that my thoughts may be fully understood. Although,

> (a) If you have any room left to be loved, Fuscus,
> Since you have friends on every side:
> I ask for one place, if any remains.
> Do not refuse me because I am new to you;
> All your old friends were once new like this:
> Only look at the one who is newly offered,
> To see if he might become an old comrade?
> *Martial, Book 1, Epigram 35.* <note>The author quotes Roman poet Martial to ask for a "place" in the reader's mind, arguing that even the oldest, most trusted ideas were once "new friends" that had to be tested.</note>

### TO THE READER.

And for this reason, I sometimes find that in those places where I have most tried to express the feelings of my mind clearly, I have nonetheless succeeded no better than **Job** in his afflictions, darkening counsel with words lacking understanding. <note>original: "Jobus... Consilia obtenebrando verbis intelligentia destitutis"; a reference to Job 38:2, where God challenges Job for speaking without full divine knowledge. The author suggests his own language may be unintentionally obscure despite his best efforts.</note> Yet I hope that the prudent, learned, and fair reader will grasp both aspects of my meaning, and will forgive those things stated awkwardly or repeated out of season.

I leave these matters to sober and wise readers to be weighed with serious and mature consideration. Let them weigh what is said, not who has said it. And may God grant them understanding in all things; so that, weary of skeptical neutrality—especially in matters of such great weight and <margin>(a)</margin> importance—they may be both able and willing, if the sum of what is said be true, to agree with these points, or otherwise, to refute them. In the meantime, until I perceive a solid reason to the contrary, I shall hope that among the "wood, hay, and stubble" <note>A reference to 1 Corinthians 3:12, where the Apostle Paul distinguishes between temporary, combustible works and those of eternal value.</note> which I may have piled up here, at least some Gold, Silver, and precious Stones—by which I mean **truths** long sought and desired— <margin>(b)</margin> will be found.

<margin>(b)</margin> One of these truths is the reconciliation of the measurements of the **New Jerusalem** with the measurements of the City mentioned in the final chapter of **Ezekiel**. This has indeed remained unknown to the Christian world until these very times.

# MR. JOSEPH MEDE’S
## Judgment concerning this Treatise.

This treatise on the Number of the Beast is the most successful of all that have come forth into the world thus far: nor can it be read (except by those who refuse to believe) without the greatest admiration. The foundation upon which it rests had been lightly touched upon by others before: namely, that this Number must be explained through a certain correspondence <note>original Greek: "ἀντιστοιχίαν"; here meaning a mathematical or symbolic symmetry</note> to the number of the Virgin company <note>A reference to the 144,000 in the Book of Revelation</note> and the New Jerusalem, by which the true and Apostolic Church is symbolically represented—all of whose numbers are derived from twelve <note>The number 12 is foundational to the New Jerusalem (12 gates, 12 foundations, etc.), just as Potter argues the number 25—the square root of 625—is foundational to the papal "Beast."</note>. But no one has ever pursued this principle to such a wonderful discovery as this author has; for he shows this Number revealing not only the particular form and character of that State which would be the Beast, but even the very City in which he was to reign, its shape and circumference, the number of its gates, of the Cardinal titles and churches, the Altar of St. Peter, and I know not how many other similar things. I first read the book with as great a prejudice against numerical speculation as could possibly be; and almost unwillingly, as I had seen so much nonsense <note>original: "nugarum"; literally trifles or "rubbish"</note> of this kind before. But when I had read it, I was moved with no less admiration than I had previously been with prejudice.

->JOSEPH MEDE.<-

<summary>Joseph Mede, a leading scholar of prophecy, provides a glowing endorsement of Potter’s work, admitting he was initially skeptical of "numerical speculation" but was won over by Potter's systematic evidence.</summary>

<keywords>Joseph Mede, Number of the Beast, New Jerusalem, Apostolic Church, 666, 12, St. Peter's Altar, biblical prophecy, 17th-century scholarship</keywords>

### Upon the Interpretation of the Apocalyptic Number 666, elaborated by FRANCIS POTTER of Oxford, with a wonderful felicity of Talent and Art, and not without a certain Divine Inspiration.

The pagan oracles fell silent immediately at the birth of the Divine Word, their mouths closed by heaven. 
As Potter opens the secrets of the Sacred Word, the mystical mind is stunned by your profane numbers, O <term>Bongus</term> <note>original: "Bonge." Pietro Bongo (Petrus Bongus) was a 16th-century Catholic theologian who wrote a famous encyclopedia on the mystical meaning of numbers. Bongo’s "Mystical Mind" was but an old wife’s tale; 
He who gave the Number <note>meaning God, the author of the Book of Revelation</note>, O Potter, gives the understanding mind to you.

Rome, you say: *“Behold, my roots are driven deep;*
*Who shall tear them out for me? Or who shall uproot them?”* you say.
Behold, a deeper Root of the Number has now unfastened them, Rome.
Soon, that root shall tear out your own roots as well.

Within the revealed Number, the hidden Beast carries 
the faint mark of a Number concealed for ages. 
The ill-omened Number and the fatal mark have been discovered; 
And at last the Beast is dragged forth from its den.

Truly, the hidden Number is the <term>Square Root</term> <note>original: *Radix Quadratica*. Potter’s primary argument was that 25 is the "root" of the number 666 (as 12 is the root of the 144,000 in the New Jerusalem), and that the number 25 permeated the structures of the Roman Church.</note> of the known figure:
Five, or rather, when repeated to the twenty-fifth.
If anyone denies this is the ill-omened number, 
he must approach Mathematics and the Sacred Page with his very first lips <note>An idiom meaning the person needs to go back to the absolute basics of both math and the Bible</note>. 
The Beast is the Roman State; which, like Romulus, 
a She-wolf nourishes; and she is both a Wild Beast and a Harlot.

for this Babylon!

# TABLE OF CONTENTS
## OF THE CHAPTERS
### *Concerning the Interpretation of the Number 666.*

#### CHAPTER I.

The likelihood of the following exposition is briefly and generally proposed: that [interpretation] which concerns the letters of names as Numbers <note>original: "literis Numeris." This refers to Gematria, the practice of assigning numerical values to letters to find names that add up to 666. Potter rejects this common method in favor of pure mathematics.</note> is almost entirely rejected. page 1

<margin>CHAP. II.</margin> The Mystery of the Number 144, which is opposed to the Number 666, consists in its own **Square Root** <note>original: "Radice quadratica." The square root of 144 is 12, which Potter connects to the 12 Apostles and the 12 tribes of Israel.</note>, which is 12. And therefore, the Mystery of the Number 666 must also be sought in its own square root. 4

<margin>CHAP. III.</margin> The method of interpreting is proposed even more clearly, though briefly. An introduction to the true interpretation of the number 144 and the measurements of the New Jerusalem <note>The "New Jerusalem" is the heavenly city described in the Book of Revelation, which Potter uses as a mathematical foil to the "Beast's" city.</note>. 9

<margin>CHAP. IV.</margin> A discussion on the Interpretation of verses 16 and 17 of Chapter 21 of the **Apocalypse** <note>The Book of Revelation.</note>. A new Exposition of the measurements of the New Jerusalem. 12

<margin>CHAP. V.</margin> A fuller confirmation of the preceding interpretation of the measurements of the New Jerusalem. 17

<margin>CHAP. VI.</margin> Interpretation of the measurement of the wall of the New Jerusalem, namely of the 144 cubits. 25

<margin>CHAP. VII.</margin> A fuller confirmation of the **cubic** and **square** measures mentioned above: demonstrating that measures of this kind are used elsewhere in the sacred scriptures. 29

<margin>CHAP. VIII.</margin> <note>The original Latin text repeats "VII" in error; this is clearly intended to be Chapter VIII.</note> The reason why the New Jerusalem is measured by such cubic and square measures. The measurement and structure of the wall, and the number by which it is expressed, typically represent the Hierarchy of the Church of Christ. Conclusion of this Digression on the measurements and number of the New Jerusalem. 33

# TABLE OF CONTENTS
## OF THE CHAPTERS
### *Regarding the Interpretation of the Number 666.*

---

#### CHAPTER I.
The probability of the following exposition is briefly and generally proposed: that which concerns numerical letters is almost entirely rejected. <note>Potter argues against "Gematria," the practice of finding names that add up to 666, preferring instead a mathematical approach based on geometry.</note> page 1

#### CHAP. II.
The mystery of the number 144, which is opposed to the number 666, consists in its **Square Root**, 12. And for that reason, the mystery of the number 666 must also be sought in its own square root. 4

#### CHAP. III.
The method of interpretation is proposed more clearly still, but cursorily. An introduction to the true interpretation of the number 144, and of the measurements of the New Jerusalem. 9

#### CHAP. IV.
An inquiry concerning the interpretation of verses 16 and 17 of Chapter 21 of the Apocalypse <note>The Book of Revelation.</note>. A new exposition of the measurements of the New Jerusalem. 12

#### CHAP. V.
A fuller confirmation of the preceding interpretation of the measurements of the New Jerusalem. 17

#### CHAP. VI.
The interpretation of the measurement of the wall of the New Jerusalem, or 144 cubits. 25

#### CHAP. VII.
A fuller confirmation of the **solid** <term>Solid: Three-dimensional measurements, such as volume or cubes.</term> and **square** measurements mentioned above: by showing that measurements of this kind are used elsewhere in the Sacred Scriptures. 29

#### CHAP. VIII.
The reason why the New Jerusalem is measured only by solid and square measurements. The measurement and structure of the wall, and the number by which it is expressed, typically represent the hierarchy of the Church of Christ. The conclusion of this digression on the measurements and numbers of the New Jerusalem. 33

<summary>This page outlines the first eight chapters of the treatise, establishing the author's thesis: the biblical numbers 144 and 666 should be understood through their square roots and geometric properties rather than simple letter-counting.</summary>

# INTERPRETATION
## OF THE NUMBER 666.

### CHAPTER I.

*The plausibility of the following exposition is briefly and generally proposed: that which concerns Numerical letters is almost entirely rejected.* <note>Numerical letters refers to the practice of "Gematria" or "Isopsephy," where scholars tried to find names whose letters added up to 666, such as "Lateinos" or "Vicarius Filii Dei." The author intends to move away from this popular method.</note>

There are indeed many and various opinions of different men, differing among themselves in their judgments and concepts, concerning that famous Number 666. Among these, however, none seems more plausible, or is more true in itself, than the opinion of those <term>Interpreters</term> <note>Biblical commentators and theologians.</note> who, rightly observing that *the same logic applies to opposites* <note>original: "Oppositorum eandem esse rationem." A classical principle of logic stating that if you understand one thing, you can understand its opposite by the same rule.</note>, have therefore attempted to investigate the true interpretation of this Number by making a comparison with that other number, 144; to which it is manifest that the number of the Beast is placed in opposition.

And this <term>Hypothesis</term> <note>The starting proposition or theory.</note> of theirs, concerning the method of interpretation, is to be considered all the more consistent with reason the less they have succeeded happily in this business. For if this method of interpretation seemed plausible to them, even though the true application of it was not yet known to them;

<vocab>Interpretation, Number 666, Beast, 144, Hypothesis</vocab>

<summary>The author introduces his study of the Number of the Beast (666) by arguing that it must be understood in direct relation to its biblical opposite, the number 144, rather than through the traditional method of calculating names from letters.</summary>

<keywords>666, 144, number of the beast, biblical interpretation, logic of opposites, Gematria, New Jerusalem</keywords>

He transitions from discussing the logic of opposites (the relationship between the numbers 144 and 666) to a specific critique of the name "Lateinos," arguing that the Bible distinguishes between a "Name" and a "Number."</meta>

<page-num>2</page-num>
<header>INTERPRETATION</header>

how much more should it be considered that they would have firmly adhered to the same [method], if they had properly perceived how many and how great mysteries were to be revealed both to themselves and to others by more closely insisting upon it.

As for that opinion concerning the numerical letters of the name *Lateinos* <note>original: "λατεῖνος." The Greek word for "Latin" or "Roman." Since the 2nd century, theologians like Irenaeus noted that the Greek letters of this name add up to 666 (L=30, A=1, T=300, E=5, I=10, N=50, O=70, S=200).</note>: although it has clear evidence regarding its true application <note>The author agrees that the "Beast" refers to the Roman/Latin power, but dislikes the mathematical method used to prove it.</note>; it is nevertheless quite uncertain and obscure regarding the method of interpretation. This is because no example exists in the Holy Scriptures where any Number has been computed in such a way, or any Name designated by such a character. What is more, the very words of the context seem to directly oppose such an interpretation concerning any Name whatsoever. Indeed, it is pointedly said, *Let him who has understanding calculate the Number of the Beast*: It is not said (as has been observed by many (a)) *calculate the Name of the Beast*, or *the numerical letters of that Name*; rather, St. John seems to have deliberately avoided this form of speaking. Thus indeed <term>Cotterius</term> <note>Matthieu Cottière, a 17th-century French Protestant theologian known for his works on the Apocalypse.</note> says, *It is most certain that John* **did not wish** *to speak in that manner.* But also, what is especially to be noted is that the Number of the Beast, and the Name of the Beast,

<margin>
(a) In my judgment, those who think the Holy Spirit is dealing with the number of the **Name** of the Antichrist wander far from the mark. *Alchafar on Revelation Chapter 13.* <note>Alchafar likely refers to Francisco Alcázar (1554–1613), a Jesuit theologian who wrote a famous commentary on the Apocalypse.</note>
If anyone has wisdom, let him calculate the Number; he makes no mention of the **Name**, but only "let him calculate the **Number** of the Beast"; and then "**and his number**" (without any mention of the **Name**) is 666. *Petrus Bungus on the mysteries of numbers, page 165.* <note>Pietro Bongo (Petrus Bungus) was a 16th-century author whose work "De Mysteriis Numerorum" was a massive compendium of number symbolism.</note>
</margin>

<meta>This page continues the argument from page 2, where the author began questioning the method of calculating a name's value (gematria) to find the identity of the Beast. He is now emphasizing that the Biblical text specifically calls for calculating a "number," not a "name," suggesting they are two distinct concepts.</meta>

<page-num>3</page-num>
<header>T H E  N U M B E R  666.</header>
<sig>A 2</sig>

the text presents [the Name and the Number] as two clearly distinct things. Therefore, it is not at all likely that the calculation of the *Name* and the calculation of the *Number* are the same thing—much less that we should calculate the *Name* instead of the *Number*. Indeed, those who have understanding are expressly advised to calculate the *Number*, not the *Name*. Although I would not entirely deny that the Holy Spirit might, in a secondary sense—indirectly and obliquely, as it were—suggest the Name of the Beast through this number, it would be highly improbable to claim that this [the Name] is the most important and primary mystery to be investigated through this number. This is something that <gloss>(a)</gloss> various learned interpreters spontaneously acknowledge along with me.

Therefore, (so far as the most important and primary intention of the passage is concerned) an interpretation should be sought that can be proven by some clear example in the Holy Scriptures; an interpretation that fits both what comes before and what follows in the context

---

(a) Because, as Jerome <note>Saint Jerome (c. 347–420 AD), the scholar and priest who translated the Bible into the Latin Vulgate.</note> wisely says regarding those words of Christ in Matthew 24, *“Let him who reads, understand,”* whenever we are challenged to understand, it shows that what is written is mystical. Similarly, in the present case, those words, *“Here is wisdom,”* do not allow the primary meaning to refer to the name of the Antichrist. *Alcazar on the 13th chapter of the Apocalypse* <note>Luis del Alcázar (1554–1613), a Spanish Jesuit theologian known for his "preterist" interpretation of Revelation.</note>. And indeed, in truth, it is not so much the number of the Beast’s name as the number of the Beast itself, as it is immediately called. Furthermore, it is called the "number of the name" only because (by God's design) it is contained within the letters of the Beast’s name when converted into numbers. *Commentary on the Apocalypse recently published at Cambridge.* The name *Lateinos* <note>original: "λατεινος." The Greek word for "Latin" or "Roman," which adds up to 666 in Greek numerals.</note> makes up the number noted by the Holy Spirit; it is a mystical number by which the Beast’s lineage is indicated. *The same [source], page 216.*

<summary>The author argues that the "Number of the Beast" should be interpreted literally as a number rather than through the "Name of the Beast" (gematria). He cites various scholars to suggest that the primary mystery of the 666 lies in its numerical properties rather than the letters of the word "Lateinos."</summary>

words, [that] might not only support but necessarily demand [this meaning]; Finally, an interpretation such that it essentially and accurately depicts that state of government to which all other marks of the Antichrist correspond: no method can be entered upon that is more likely, or more in accordance with reason; none that is less opposed to the writings of the leading interpreters, than that we should stand upon the foundations already laid by those who have busied themselves investigating the mystery hinted at in this number, by making a comparison with the number 144 <note>The number 144 (144,000) appears in the Book of Revelation (chapters 7 and 14) as the number of the redeemed "servants of God."</note>, to which this [number] 666 is opposed as a kind of *Anti-number* <note>original: "Anti-numerus"</note>: and therefore it must be explained in the same way as that other [number], and thus adapted in every detail to the Synagogue of the Antichrist <note>The author uses "Synagogue" here in the sense of a gathering or assembly, contrasting the institutional body of the Antichrist against the Church of Christ.</note>, just as that other 144 is explained and adapted in the Holy Scriptures to the Church of Christ.

### Chapter II.

> *The mystery of the Number 144, opposed to the number 666, consists in its <term>square root<gloss>original: Radice quadratica</gloss></term>, 12. And therefore, the mystery of the Number 666 must also be sought in its own square root.*

Now, concerning the manner in which the Number 144 is to be interpreted, there is already agreement by a nearly unanimous consensus of all interpreters, both ancient and modern. Namely, why this number was chosen above all others for the [measuring] of the Wall... <note>The text likely refers to the "measuring of the wall" of the New Jerusalem in Revelation 21:17, which is 144 cubits.</note>

-># THE NUMBER 666.<-

->A 3<-

...the measure of the heavenly Jerusalem <note>original: "Hierosolymæ"; a reference to the city described in Revelation 21, measured in multiples of twelve.</note>; that this is the sole cause, or at least the most significant one: that this assembly traces its origin to, and is built upon, the Number 12; which, multiplied by itself, produces the <term>square number<gloss>A number produced by multiplying a whole number by itself.</gloss></term> 144.

For just as the square number *one hundred forty-four* rises and is constructed from the number twelve alone, and cannot admit any other number as its <term>Root and Base<gloss>original: Radice & Base</gloss></term>; (which is clearly evident to all those instructed in <term>Arithmetic<gloss>The branch of mathematics dealing with the properties and manipulation of numbers.</gloss></term> at least enough to calculate numbers and extract their square roots <note>original: "quadraticas Radices"</note>); so too the Church of Christ admits no other Foundation than that which has already been laid in the *Twelve* Apostles. Just as, therefore, this Number 144 is constructed upon 12 units: so the Church of Christ is built upon the 12 Apostles.

And just as the number 12 is more conspicuous and notable in the number 144 than any other; as it measures not only its base and root, but also all its sides and rows in every direction (which will be clearly evident to anyone considering and calculating the sides and units of the square figure described here, where the number 144 is arranged in proper order, with the same number of units placed <term>perpendicularly<gloss>original: normaliter; meaning at right angles to form a perfect square.</gloss></term>, and at equal distances from one another); just as, I say, in this <term>figurate number<gloss>A number that can be represented by a geometric pattern of dots; here, a 12x12 grid.</gloss></term>, composed of 144 Units, the number 12 is, above all others...

<page-num>7</page-num>

### THE NUMBER 666.

->A 4<-

...distinguished and notable than any other number whatsoever.

And it is for this reason that this number 12 occurs more than one hundred and forty-four times in Holy Scripture; and in so many, and such diverse ways, in matters pertaining to the Church, it is employed by the Holy Spirit, that we cannot but recognize this number as having been selected, as it were, and preferred by the Holy Spirit above all others.

And although the number 144 can truly be called the Number of God <note>original: "Dei Numerus"</note> in a more peculiar way than many others used in the Holy Scriptures—because it represents the Figure of the City <note>A reference to the New Jerusalem in Revelation 21:17, where the wall is measured at 144 cubits.</note>, and generally the form and structure of the Church and its Hierarchy <term>Hierarchy|The organized body of ecclesiastical officials; here the author views the Church as a structured building or order.</term> (as will be discussed later)—nevertheless it is not so properly called the number of God as is the number 12. For 12 is applied to the Church in almost every more significant consideration; and it is always used in Holy Scripture as a *definite number for a definite thing, not as a definite number for an indefinite thing.* <note>In biblical interpretation, some numbers are "indefinite" (like 'seven' meaning 'many'), but the author argues 12 always refers to a specific, literal foundation, like the 12 Tribes or 12 Apostles.</note>

And with a view to this, it must be entirely conceded that the number 144 signifies and represents the Church in a general sense. Indeed, it is not in itself, and considered collectively (as the number 12 is), applicable to any particular times, or persons, or places, or other specific things mentioned in Holy Scripture; but only with respect had to its Root or Base <term>Root or Base|In mathematical terms, the 'root' is the number which, when multiplied by itself, produces the square. Here, 12 is the square root of 144.</term>, which is the num-

<summary>The author argues that while 144 represents the Church's general structure, the number 12 is the primary "Number of God" because of its frequent and specific use throughout Scripture as a foundational constant.</summary>

number 12. For indeed the Tribes were 12, not 144; there were 12 gates of Jerusalem, not 144; likewise the Apostles were 12, not 144. And the same must be said of many other things.

And indeed, since the number 144 is mentioned nowhere else in Holy Scripture except in Chapter 21 of the Book of Revelation <note>original: "Apocalypfeos"</note>, it must be entirely granted that even there, the measure of the Wall—by which the spiritual building of the Church of God is implied in that place—is not designated by this number because there were then, or ever would be, exactly that many and no more faithful Christians (or "living stones" <note>A reference to 1 Peter 2:5, where believers are described as "living stones" being built into a spiritual house.</note>), built upon the twelve foundations mentioned there. Rather, we should learn from this that however large or small the number of faithful Christians might be, they are all nonetheless built upon the foundation of the 12 Apostles, just as the number 144 is built upon 12 units.

And from this it is clear (as most interpreters freely admit) that the number 144 was selected for the measure of the Wall of the New Jerusalem for this sole reason, or at least this primary one: that it is the only square number <term>square number|A number produced by multiplying a number by itself; in this case, 12 multiplied by 12 equals 144.</term> which can be raised or constructed upon 12 units. This is perfectly clear to all who have an understanding of extracting a square root <term>square root|The factor of a square number; the author argues that 12 is the "root" or foundational source from which the 144 cubits of the heavenly city are derived.</term>.

Here, it first comes to be considered that the **Stadia** and **Cubits** <note>A "stadium" (plural: stadia) was an ancient Greek unit of distance, roughly 600 feet. Therefore, it must be carefully weighed and investigated how many kinds of measuring by Stadia and Cubits have been employed by men. Indeed, no reason can be conceived why those words—*the measure of a man, which is that of an Angel*—should be added, other than to serve as an explanation for what came before, and to remove the doubts and hesitations that might arise from them. It is probable, therefore, that the true interpretation of these words could be like a key to open up those things which had been said about the measures just listed.

Now, if it had been simply said, *These cubits are the cubits of a man*, or *these Stadia are the stadia of a man*; it would be entirely probable that these words were added so that there would be no error regarding the proper length of the Stadia or cubits. However, since it is said, "The measure of a man, which is that of an Angel," <note>original Greek: "Μέτρον ἀνθρώπου ὅ ἐστιν Ἀγγέλου" (*Metron anthrōpou ho estin Angelou*).</note> these words seem to suggest that the danger of erring lies rather in this: that they might wrongly understand the *method* of measuring by Stadia

If this is the case, then the method of measuring suggested here is not the one that is most frequently used by men or encountered in the Scriptures. It appears, therefore, that the kind of measuring by **Stadia** and **Cubits** <note>A stadium was an ancient Greek unit of distance, roughly 600 feet. A cubit was based on the length of a forearm, approximately 18 inches.</note> which the Angel <note>referring to the heavenly messenger measuring the New Jerusalem in Revelation 21</note> uses in this passage is of a type that properly belongs to man, yet is not the one most commonly used either among men or in the Holy Scriptures. Therefore, to investigate what this measuring method is—one that is less common but most appropriate here—we must carefully inquire into how many ways men use stadia, cubits, or similar measures to calculate size.

To answer this question, there are exactly three—and no more—types of measuring that we must consider. For just as there are only three distinct types of things to be measured (commonly called the *Line*, the *Surface*, and the *Body* <note>original: "Linea, Superficies, & Corpus." In modern terms: length, area, and volume.</note>), there are likewise three types of measures used by men that correspond properly to these three types of quantity: namely, **Linear** measure, **Superficial** <note>area</note> measure, and **Solid** <note>volume</note> measure. Without these measures, the quantities just mentioned can neither be properly expressed nor properly understood.

From the very words of the text, therefore, and from the kind of measurement that properly belongs to men and is accepted by them in common use, it is entirely concluded that the measure we have here of 12,000 **Stadia** <term>Stadia: Plural of stadium, an ancient Greek unit of distance (approx. 600 feet). This is because there is absolutely no way of measuring solid figures—neither one possible in itself, nor one accepted in human usage—except through solid measures. For it is impossible for a man to seek out or know the true quantity of a solid body either **Intuitively** <note>original: "Intuitive." In scholastic philosophy, this refers to immediate knowledge without the need for reasoning, a power attributed to angels.</note>, as the **Angels** do, or **Applicatively** <note>original: "Applicative." This refers to measuring something by physically applying a tool to it, like a ruler to a line.</note>, as with linear measures. Instead, man knows volume only **Discursively** <note>original: "Discursive." This refers to the human process of reasoning step-by-step, such as using a mathematical formula to calculate volume rather than "seeing" it all at once.</note> and through reasoning—that is, by the help of the discursive faculty, and by computing numbers or reducing them to calculations. Since this is a work of human reason alone, it is in this very place, for this reason alone or primarily, that it is called the **measure of a man**. <note>The author is interpreting Revelation 21:17: "And he measured the wall thereof... according to the measure of a man, that is, of the angel."</note>

Concerning which city, as well as the heavenly Jerusalem, the words of *Villalpando* <note>Juan Bautista Villalpando (1552–1608), a Spanish Jesuit scholar and architect famous for his massive commentary on Ezekiel, where he used geometry to reconstruct the Temple.</note> in *book 2, chapter 21, page 118, on Ezekiel chapter 48*, are plausible and worthy of note. Where, concerning the City described by Ezekiel, he writes: *“In which place it ought to be a doubt to no one that mention is made of the new Jerusalem, once to be founded from the side of Christ, but now indeed founded upon the foundation of the Apostles and Prophets, with Jesus Christ himself as the chief cornerstone; but that it reflected in all things the arrangement, parts, and their names and* **MEASUREMENTS** *of the ancient City, can in no way be a doubt to anyone who weighs these things even moderately.”*

By these words, Villalpando confidently affirms two things. First, that the City described by the Prophet Ezekiel is the same as the new Jerusalem of which St. John speaks. ...[looked back] not only to the shape, names, and parts of that ancient and literal Jerusalem, but also to its *Measurements*. For if Saint *John* and the Prophet *Ezekiel* describe the heavenly Jerusalem in that manner—having a square shape, twelve gates, the names of the twelve tribes, and the names of the twelve Apostles—since these very things once existed in reality and in act within the literal Jerusalem, why indeed should there not be an equal reason and a foundation of truth that this very measure of 12,000 stadia <term>stadia: an ancient Greek unit of length, approximately 600 feet; 12,000 stadia is roughly 1,400 miles</term> was sought and derived from such measures as once existed in that ancient and literal Jerusalem?

I say, therefore; if that City described by *Ezekiel* is the same as this new Jerusalem, then this new Jerusalem must also agree with the description provided there, not only in its shape and the number of gates and tribes, but also in its measurement and circumference.

And since the circumference of the City in the last chapter of *Ezekiel* is expressly stated to be 18,000 cubits <term>cubit: an ancient unit of length based on the distance from the elbow to the tip of the middle finger, roughly 18 inches</term>, it is manifest that this measurement of 12,000 stadia cannot be understood as a linear measurement, whether of one side or of the entire circumference of the New Jerusalem. ### THE NUMBER 666.

->B 3<-

Therefore, it cannot be that the 12,000 stadia <term>stadia: an ancient unit of length; 12,000 stadia is roughly 1,380 miles</term> are to be understood as the length of one side or as the entire circumference of the New Jerusalem.

In a similar way, if anyone wishes (as I see some Interpreters have desired) that these 12,000 stadia be understood as the measurement of the surface area of the New Jerusalem’s square: it would be necessary for that person to make the circumference at least 436 stadia, or even greater (which will be clearly evident by extracting the square root of the number 12,000 <note>The square root of 12,000 is approximately 109.5; a square with sides of 109.5 would have a perimeter of roughly 438</note>). But we have already shown that the circumference of the city described by *Ezekiel* cannot be greater than 144 stadia. Therefore, these 12,000 stadia cannot be understood as the measurement of the surface area, whether of the New Jerusalem itself or of one of its sides.

It remains, therefore, to be said that if this city’s measurement is to agree with the one measured by *Ezekiel* (which seems necessary to conclude), then these 12,000 stadia must be understood as a **solid measurement** <term>solid measurement: a calculation of volume (cubic measure) rather than flat area or length</term>. Indeed, only through this approach will it be possible to reconcile these two different measurements of the same city. It is necessary, then, for a city whose solid measurement is 12,000 solid stadia to have a circumference of 18,000 cubits <term>cubit: an ancient unit of length, roughly 18 to 21 inches</term> according to the measurement described by *Ezekiel*.

However, so that this may be established, something must be said regarding the length of *Ezekiel’s* cubits and *John’s* stadia. Regarding the cubit used by *Ezekiel* in describing his final vision,

# INTERPRETATION

...then, it is clear from chapter 40, verse 5; chapter 41, verse 8; and chapter 43, verse 13 <note>These verses in the Book of Ezekiel describe the measurements of the visionary Temple and city</note>, that it is one palm longer than the one usually employed elsewhere in the Holy Scriptures; that is, it is larger by a fourth part of an ordinary cubit <term>cubit: an ancient unit of length based on the distance from the elbow to the tip of the middle finger</term>, which <gloss>(a)</gloss> Villalpandus <note>Juan Bautista Villalpando (1552–1608) was a Spanish Jesuit scholar and architect famous for his massive commentary on Ezekiel and his reconstructed drawings of the Temple of Solomon</note> and other Interpreters affirm, not without reason, regarding that passage of Ezekiel. Furthermore, the common or ordinary cubit mentioned throughout the scriptures was approximately two and a half feet. Accordingly, in some editions of the English translation of the Bible, a marginal note equates 2,000 cubits to one mile. (Villalpandus also notes this in his description of Jerusalem, titled *A True Image of Old Jerusalem, published in Rome with the permission of the Superiors, as well as by the privilege of the Supreme Pontiff, the Emperor, the Catholic King, and the Venetian Senate, etc.*) Moreover, a mile is 1,000 paces, and any pace is five feet.

If, therefore, the cubit of Ezekiel is a fourth part larger than the other cubit, it follows from this that 22,500 ordinary cubits equal 18,000 of the larger cubits of Ezekiel. (Indeed, as 4 is to 5, so 18,000 is to 22,500.) If, then, 22,500 ordinary cubits (each of which is two and a half feet) is the true circumference of the New Jerusalem measured by Ezekiel; and if one stadium <term>stadium: a Greek unit of distance; here defined as 625 feet</term> is 625 feet; it follows that the circumference of the entire city is 90 stadia.

How properly this measurement indeed agrees with the solid measurement <note>The "solid measurement" refers to the volume or cubic capacity of the city, rather than just its perimeter</note> described by Saint John...

# THE NUMBER 666.

->B 4<-

...it is shown, by extracting the cube root <term>cube root: the number that, when multiplied by itself twice (e.g., x * x * x), produces a given number</term> of the number 12,000 (which is slightly less than 22 8/10). From this calculation, the perimeter of the city is found to be greater than 91 stadia <term>stadium: an ancient Greek unit of distance, roughly 600 feet or 1/8th of a Roman mile</term>, but less than 92 (specifically, it is nearly 91 1/3).

And although this measurement does not exactly and precisely agree with the one from Ezekiel <note>The prophet Ezekiel describes a visionary city in Ezekiel 48, which the author is comparing to the New Jerusalem in Revelation</note>, a difference of only one or two stadia in such a large circumference is negligible. Yet, it is not simply to be ignored because the difference is small. Rather, it is far more likely that these circumferences match exactly—since they describe the same city—than it is possible for anyone today to precisely determine the length of the Jewish cubit <term>Jewish cubit: a specific biblical unit of measurement</term> or the Roman stadium, and therefore the exact ratio between them.

Indeed, it would be a mark of great ignorance to claim that the true length of either measurement could be proven today by any ancient monument of undisputed reliability or by any unquestionable authority. Because nothing in this earthly world <note>original: "sublunari mundo", literally "the world beneath the moon," a traditional way of describing the mortal, changing realm of Earth</note> is immortal or unchanging—except perhaps for the written word—and nothing can be passed down to future generations without some degree of alteration, it has always been considered impossible, even up to our own time, to find any demonstrably certain method to preserve the exact length of a measurement across the centuries without some noticeable error occurring.

# INTERPRETATION

But truly, although the exact measure of the *Jewish* cubit <term>Jewish cubit: an ancient biblical unit of length, roughly 18–22 inches, though its precise standard was a subject of much debate among early modern scholars</term> is uncertain and buried in oblivion, the *Roman* stadium and the *Roman* foot are not so entirely unknown that we cannot approach the truth; just as *Snellius* <note>Willebrord Snellius (1580–1626) was a Dutch astronomer and mathematician famous for his work on measuring the Earth’s circumference</note> seems to have defined in his book *On the Circumference of the Earth*. And, assuming that 22,500 cubits (which is the perimeter of the city measured by *Ezekiel*) equals 57,235 or 57,236 *Roman* feet (which the perimeter of the New *Jerusalem* described by *St. John* <note>The author of the Book of Revelation</note> closely approaches), there arises from this a length for the *Jewish* cubit of two feet and a half, plus a little more than half of one inch. And so, I believe, the true length of the *Jewish* cubit will be better understood in this way than by any other method.

I therefore consider that these reasons already mentioned, if they are correctly understood, abundantly show that it is not only probable but even necessary that this measure of 12,000 stadia be understood as the solid <term>solid measure: a measurement of volume rather than just a linear distance along a single side</term> measurement of the city. Furthermore, this measurement—as (ª) *Andreas of Caesarea* <note>A 6th-century bishop and influential commentator on the Book of Revelation</note> also rightly conjectures—is not to be applied linearly to the city's perimeter or height until human reason (by the help of the discursive faculty <note>original: "facultatis discursivae," referring to the mind's ability to move logically from one premise to another</note>, whose duty this is) resolves this number into others, or unless it is entirely necessary to extract the cube root. And so much for what has been said regarding the measurement of the city.

### CHAPTER VI.

->*Interpretation of the measurement of the wall of the New Jerusalem, or 144 cubits.*<-

The measurement of the wall follows, which in the following words is said to be 144 cubits.

This measurement indeed cannot be understood as the length of the wall: for it is impossible that 144 cubits <term>cubit: an ancient unit of length based on the distance from the elbow to the tip of the middle finger, approximately 18 to 22 inches</term> could surround that city, whose perimeter has already been shown to be 91 stadia <note>original: "stadiorum 91." This refers to the author's previous calculation where the city's side—the cube root of 12,000—is approximately 22.89 stadia; thus, four sides equal roughly 91 stadia.</note>. And indeed, even if it were possible, it would be a certain tautology <term>tautology: a logical redundancy where the same thing is said twice in different words</term> to describe the length of the wall in these words, which could certainly and clearly be known from the measurement of the city already explained.

Therefore, this measurement must be understood as either the height of the wall, or its thickness, or both at once. Its meaning will be either that the wall is 144 cubits high, or 144 cubits wide; or, according to both measurements of height and thickness <note>The author likely means "thickness" here, though the Latin "longitudinis" (length/extension) is used, implying the cross-section</note>, it would be 144 square cubits. And indeed, this last interpretation of the words, although it may seem intricate and unusual to those who are not accustomed to such measurements, or who have not noted this kind of measurement clearly and explicitly mentioned in other places in Holy Scripture: nevertheless, it is according to this type of measurement alone that walls are usually measured by builders. And that this kind of measurement—understand it as the square <term>square measurement: calculating the area of a surface (like the face of a wall) rather than just a linear line</term>—is to be understood in this place...

...to be preferred, I think, as more than probable for the following reasons.

First, that the measurement of the wall which should be accepted above others (all things being equal <note>original: "cæteris paribus"</note>) is the one by which the wall’s quantity—which is otherwise still unknown—can be more perfectly known and discerned according to more dimensions. But assuming that the 144 cubits are the **square measurement** of the wall, representing height and thickness together, it follows from this that not only the height, nor only the thickness, but all the wall's dimensions that were previously unknown can be discovered by this number.

On the contrary, if these 144 cubits are understood as the thickness of the wall, the height remains unknown; if they are understood as the height of the wall, the thickness remains unknown. And even if it were certain that they were to be understood as one of these single measurements, it would still remain ambiguous and impossible to determine from the words of the text which one was meant. Furthermore, even if it were known which of the two measurements was intended, the shape and proportion of the wall would still remain unknown.

But if, as has just been said, this measurement is to be understood as **square cubits**; all these uncertainties and ambiguities immediately vanish and are avoided; and it will then be manifest that this single number reveals the wall’s shape as well as both of those dimensions which were otherwise unknown. For just as this is a square number <note>A "square number" in this context refers to the product of a number multiplied by itself; 144 is the square of 12.</note> having four sides equal to one another, each of which consists of 12 units, so too in the wall it is to be con-

### CHAPTER VII.

*A fuller confirmation of the solid and square measures mentioned above: by demonstrating that such measures are used elsewhere in the Sacred Scriptures.*

One task still remains for the clearer evidence of the measures already mentioned; namely, to show that in other places of Holy Scripture, such measures and forms of speech are employed by the Holy Spirit.

If therefore ten cubits were the measure of one stone's length, or width, or thickness; it would have to be granted (provided they were square on all sides, that is, in a cubic form) that one stone would be 25 feet long, 25 feet wide, and 25 feet high, and thus contain 15,625 solid feet <note>original: "pedes solidos," what we would today call cubic feet</note> of solid stone; which indeed is more than 240 wagons could carry. But it is altogether incredible, and foreign to all reason, and contrary to historical truth itself, that these stones were of such stupendous magnitude. Moreover, it would follow from this that the wall, which was constructed from three rows of these stones, was more than 20 feet thick and more than 60 feet high. These things are indeed so foreign to historical truth, and discordant with all reason, that I believe no one would be so foolishly credulous as to think so. It remains, therefore, that those numbers be understood as <term>solid measure</term> <note>A volumetric measurement, such as cubic cubits, rather than a linear measurement of one side.</note>; according to which stones and timber are accustomed to be measured in common usage. According to this measure, a cubic stone of eight cubits <note>Meaning a stone with a total volume of 8 cubic cubits.</note> will be two cubits long, wide, and high <note>Because 2 x 2 x 2 = 8. This makes the stones roughly 5 feet in each dimension—a much more realistic size for ancient masonry.</note>. And three rows of such stones, along with one row of cedar beams, would constitute a wall of credible and convenient height and thickness, fitting for those walls mentioned in 1 Kings, chapter 6, verse 36, and chapter 7, verses 10, 11, and 12.

# INTERPRETATION

[*square*] <note>This completes the sentence from the previous page: "...the whole offering shall be twenty-five thousand by twenty-five thousand, square."</note> *—a fact that both commends the mathematical disciplines and clearly demonstrates that a student of Theology should not be entirely ignorant of them.*

If, in this passage from *Ezekiel*, the Holy Spirit uses a <term>circumlocution</term> <note>A roundabout way of speaking.</note> so that He might express and suggest the <term>square or flat measure</term> <note>The area.</note> of a field through a geometric phrase—by multiplying a number by itself—why could He not also, in another place, describe that same square measure with a single number, without any wordy explanation? If in the first passage, the sides of the square are given, yet the square number itself is not expressed, but left for the reader to find by multiplying the sides (as *St. Jerome* <note>Eusebius Sophronius Hieronymus (c. 347–420 AD), the translator of the Vulgate Bible.</note> did in this instance), why could the square number itself not be given in another passage of Scripture where the sides are not expressed? There, it would be left to the reader to investigate by <term>extracting the square root</term> <note>Finding the number that, when multiplied by itself, produces the given total (the "radix quadratica").</note> of the number.

Just as it would have been unnecessary for the square number to be explicitly told to *Ezekiel*, because it can be established with certainty that the <term>square measure</term> of that sacred offering is 625 million square cubits—or 667 square miles, as *St. Jerome* calculates—simply by multiplying 25,000 by itself: so too it was unnecessary for the linear measures of the wall to be explicitly told to *St. John* <note>The author of the Book of Revelation.</note> by the Angel. ### CHAPTER VIII.

*The reason why the New Jerusalem is measured only by solid and square measures. The conclusion of this digression concerning the measurements and numbers of the New Jerusalem.*

If it is asked for what reason the Angel did not present only linear measurements <note>Measurements of length or distance (one-dimensional) rather than area or volume.</note> of this New Jerusalem—just as was done in the visions of *Ezekiel* and other places of Holy Scripture where descriptions of this kind occur:

I would not dare to accuse men of such great learning of this kind of lack of knowledge, much less of ignorance.

Indeed, the extraction of a root from numbers is a human invention, and perhaps even a Pagan one. But it is a lawful and useful invention. It is the basis and foundation for many things in both **Arithmetic** and **Geometry**; and it is such a necessary, if not essential, part of those sciences that without it, neither of them could easily stand. From this was investigated that famous discovery for which **Pythagoras** is said to have sacrificed a <term>Hecatomb</term> <note>A massive public sacrifice, literally of 100 oxen; the author refers to the legend that Pythagoras sacrificed these to the gods after discovering his famous theorem ($a^2 + b^2 = c^2$).</note> to the Gods. Certainly, if Divine wisdom will one day deign to unlock the mystery of this Number through human inventions (which the very words of Scripture seem to suggest), it seems that this should be expected from no other method more than this one; by whose aid many noble mysteries were revealed in the past, and continue to be revealed daily.

# INTERPRETATION

...differs most distinctly <note>original: *tiffimum*, the conclusion of the word *distinctissimum* from the previous page.</note> in this respect; namely, that it asserts the primary mystery does not consist in the application of this very Number 666 to the Antichrist, but in seeking out, through its calculation, another Number which is most properly and notably applicable to him. I have judged it altogether necessary not to pass over those arguments which the text itself provides for a fuller confirmation of this point, in which the difference consists. For although it cannot be denied that a similar interpretation of the opposing Number <note>Referring to the number 144, whose square root is 12.</note> (as was said above) is a valid and powerful presumption that the Number of the Beast should be interpreted similarly: yet the words of the text themselves are so fitting, and prove this interpretation so clearly, that I do not see how it can be avoided, even if no other example existed in Holy Scripture <note>original: *Sacra Scriptura*.</note> by which it could be proven.

<meta>This page completes the sentence started on page 42 ("Let him who is endowed with understanding") and begins the detailed argument for why the calculation of the Number of the Beast follows human logic rather than divine or angelic mystery.</meta>

# THE NUMBER 666.

*...is endowed [with understanding], let him calculate the Number of the Beast: for it is the number of a man: and his number is six hundred sixty-six.* <note>This text completes the quotation of Revelation 13:18 from the previous page: "Here is wisdom. Let him who is endowed with understanding..."</note>

In these words, it is expressly stated that the Number 666 must be calculated. Now, according to what method should this Number be calculated, if not according to one of those methods in common use among men? And lest anyone should perhaps understand this as referring to some purely speculative calculation, or an Angelic one, or some intricate and unusual kind of counting; it is immediately added, *for it is the number of a man*. This is (as (ª) Alcázar <note>Luis del Alcázar (1554–1613), a Spanish Jesuit theologian.</note>, Cottière <note>Matthieu Cottière, a 17th-century French Protestant minister.</note>, (ᵇ) Gaspar à Melo <note>A Portuguese Augustinian friar and commentator.</note>, and (ᶜ) many other interpreters explain) not only such a Number, but also such a method of calculation, as is known to men and commonly used among people.

---

(a) *Here is wisdom* <note>original: "Hic sapientia"</note>, denotes that there is a need for sharpness of mind and insight to perceive the things that follow. The construction of Richard <note>Likely Richard of St. Victor, a 12th-century mystic and scholar.</note> is appropriate; namely, that it is called the *Number of a man* because it is a number that can be observed by a human; it is as if it were said that the method of enumeration and calculation is of a kind that the power of human intellect can achieve and undertake. By this reasoning, the phrase is neither unusual nor the logic inept; indeed, the connection is most fitting: *He who has wisdom, let him calculate the Number of the Beast, for this calculation can be performed by the powers of the human intellect*—*for it is the number of a man* (1) because it is a reckoning of the sort that can be completed in a human manner. *Alcázar on Revelation chapter 13.* (b) *For it is the number of a man* (1) as Aretas <note>Aretas of Caesarea, a 10th-century commentator on the Apocalypse.</note> says, it is a customary calculation and known among men; or otherwise: He who has understanding, let him calculate well, for it is proper to a man possessing understanding to count. *Gaspar à Melo on Revelation chapter 21.* (c) For he says *it is the number of a man*, that is, as is said elsewhere regarding another matter: *And he measured its Wall, 144 Cubits, by the Measure of a man, which is that of an Angel* <note>A reference to Revelation 21:17, where human and angelic measurements are equated.</note>—Thus when he says simply *it is the number of a man*, he understands that a human being is capable of both receiving and understanding this Number. *Anonymous writer in Commentaries on the Apocalyptic Beast, pages 140 and 141. Bodleian Library, Oxford. [Shelfmark] M. 12. 16. Theology.*

<summary>This page argues that the "Number of the Beast" (666) must be calculated using standard human mathematical methods rather than supernatural ones. For the fact that the Antichrist will be a future man serves no purpose in determining whether his Number is calculated or not.”* Thus far *Alcázar* on Revelation 13. From his words, it should be observed that the very evidence of the truth rendered him so confident that he dared to refute that interpretation of the words which almost all other <term>Papists</term> embrace.

And at this point (always maintaining due reverence for better judgments), I hope it will be permitted to me, without giving offense to others, to briefly set forth what seems to me the most probable opinion concerning the two Beasts and the Image of the Beast mentioned in chapter 13 of Revelation.

# NUMERI 666.

->temporal<- sovereignty of the Roman Emperors, which from that time was usurped by the Popes.

For I believe that the Bishop and the Roman Clergy—shortly after the time of **Constantine the Great** <note>Constantine I (r. 306–337), the first Roman Emperor to convert to Christianity.</note>, whether by his own donation or through their own usurpation when the Emperor was absent or removed—occupied and usurped (at least for a short time) temporal sovereignty in the city of Rome and its annexed territories, and over the people themselves. I believe that this temporal sovereignty, and the riches and wealth which the Popes and the Roman Clergy then possessed, was the beginning of their greatness. *For there is not just one system for numbers; we measure our own affairs by systems of tens, hundreds, thousands, and ten-thousands; why then should Angels not follow other arrangements of Numbers? Since this Number, then, is the **Number of a man** <note>original: "Numerus hominis" — This refers to Revelation 13:18: "Let him that hath understanding count the number of the beast: for it is the number of a man; and his number is Six hundred threescore and six."</note>; that is, a Number of the same kind as those used by us humans, the inhabitants of this world; it is not to be doubted that its calculation is the same sort of calculation used among us men.*

I ask, therefore: what does it mean to calculate a Number in the manner of men? And what could be the literal and grammatical sense of these words, unless they are understood as referring to such a calculation as is both common among men and proper to Numbers?

Indeed, there is no other way in which men are commonly accustomed, or can properly be said, to calculate Numbers, except by one of these methods (or what can be reduced to one of these): namely, by **Adding**, **Subtracting**, **Multiplying**, **Dividing**, or **Extracting the Root**. <note>The author is listing the fundamental operations of arithmetic to argue that the mystery of 666 must be solved using one of them—specifically hinting at the extraction of the square root, which was a central part of this author's specific theory.</note> It is therefore altogether necessary that the **Number of the Beast** <note>original: "Numerus Bestiæ"</note> must be calculated according to one of these methods. Furthermore, in all these modes of calculating, and in each one of them, the intention and goal is this: that, given one or more Numbers, another may be discovered which was previously unknown, and which could not be expressed until the calculation was performed. ### CHAPTER XI.

*What it is to extract the square root of a Number. The Number 25 is the Root of the Number 666; and is notably opposed to the Number 144. Certain doubts are answered concerning the small fractions adhering to the Root of the Number 666.*

To extract the <term>square root<note>original: "Radicem quadraticam"; the mathematical process of finding a number which, when multiplied by itself, produces the original number.</note></term> of a Number is to find the greatest Number which, when multiplied by itself (with the addition of the remainder, if there be any), produces the stated Number.
