A military tactic where troops are arranged in a block with an equal number of rows (ranks) and columns (files).</note></term> with equal intervals: to achieve this, the <term>square Root<note>original: "Radix quadratica"</note></term> of the Number 666 must be extracted, so that he may know how many ranks there will be, and how many soldiers are to be placed in each rank. The remaining 41 soldiers, however, must be ignored as useless for the present matter, provided he wants the formation to be perfectly square. Similarly, the Number 50 is not a perfect square: but *St. Augustine* (in his commentary on Psalm 150, and elsewhere) wishes its mystery to consist in its Root, 7; having no concern for the fraction that ought to be added. <note>Augustine argues that 50 represents the "jubilee" or perfection, and relates it to 7x7 (49). # THE NUMBER 666.

...we should take the <term>Whole Number<note>original: "Integer"</note></term> 25 by itself (neglecting the Fraction) as the Root of the Number 666, corresponding on the opposite side to the Number 12: especially since the units of this Root must sometimes be applied to Persons, who are, by their nature, things that are <term>indivisible<note>The author is pointing out the practical absurdity of having a fraction of a person when calculating the "root" of a group of people.</note></term> into parts.

Indeed, the Units of any Number, considered essentially and absolutely, are to be held as indivisible; nor should any Fraction of a Number, as a fraction, properly be considered a part of a numerical Root when considered in its essence. For however true it may be that Fractions, when reduced to a certain <term>denomination<note>original: "denominationem," referring to the denominator of a fraction.</note></term>, more accurately show the side of a **square Figure, insofar as it is a continuous quantity**; nevertheless, it cannot be proved that these same fractions are, properly speaking, parts of a **Numerical Root, insofar as it is a discrete quantity**. 

<note>The author uses the Aristotelian distinction between "continuous quantity" (geometry, where lines can be divided infinitely) and "discrete quantity" (arithmetic, where units like people or stones are counted as whole things).</note>

For a Fraction added to a Root supposes every Unit of the Whole to be broken down into as many parts as there are in the Fraction's Denominator; and it supposes that the Number itself from which the Root is extracted is broken down into another Number much larger than itself. And the Fraction itself (if we consider the matter rightly) is rather a part of the Root of that larger Number into which the first proposed Number is presumed to be broken down, rather than a part of the Root of that first Number which was to be extracted.

For example: when the Root of the Number 666 is said to be 25 and 806/1000; this Fraction supposes the number 666 to be multiplied by a thousand thousands <note>i.e., one million</note>; and each of the Units of the Root 25 to be multiplied by a thousand; which Fra-

### THE NUMBER 666

[...Indeed, with] some variety of Fractions, it becomes the Root of many Numbers; this is a matter which those who are not accustomed to the **extractions of Roots** neither notice nor sufficiently understand. And this is why they believe that, because 25 is the Root of the Number 625, it therefore cannot be the Root of the Number 666. 

But they should consider that this is the sole reason why Numbers of this kind are called "Roots": because each of them is (in this regard) like the **Root of a Tree**. For, just as a single root carries many branches growing out of it—though some are closer to the root than others—in the same way, the same Number is the Root of many numbers which actually arise from it, albeit with some degree of variation. And although there is no infallible direction from the root upward as to which branch one might follow, yet when going downward from the tip of any branch, there is a certain and infallible direction back to that same Root. 

In like manner, whoever goes to extract the Root of any Number that is greater than 624 and less than 676 <note>25 squared is 625; 26 squared is 676. A cardinal number is a counting number like 1, 2, 3, as opposed to an ordinal number like 1st, 2nd, 3rd.</note> in every extraction of Roots, as the most important and primary objective.

### INTERPRETATION

It is neither customary nor convenient to express any Root that has an attached fraction by any other **Whole Number** than by that which, when multiplied by itself, produces the largest square number contained within the number whose root is proposed to be extracted. And although there are many numbers besides 666 through which we might be certainly directed to 25 as the unique **Whole Number** by which their root should be expressed, none of them—except 666 alone—possesses a most perfect figure representing the figure of Rome; just as the most perfect figure of the number 144 represents the figure of Jerusalem. <note>The author, likely Francis Potter, is linking the "Number of the Beast" (666) to the city of Rome by finding its square root (25), just as the square root of 144 (the number of the New Jerusalem in Revelation) is 12. He views these mathematical roots as the foundational "figures" or characters of these cities.</note> And for this primary reason—as well as for others (which will be explained more fully in their proper place later)—it was both fitting and necessary that this number 666 be chosen above all others.

If it is objected that the true root of the number 666 comes closer to 26 than to 25 <note>The square root of 666 is approximately 25.806. Mathematically, it is indeed closer to 26 than 25, but the author argues that in the "Art" of root extraction, the integer part (25) remains the defining root.</note>, I answer that the root should not for that reason be called 26, but is truly called 25. This is so not only because the number 25 is contained within the root (which is also true of all numbers smaller than 25), but because 25 is the greatest number of **Units** of the same denomination as 666 that is contained within the root itself.

<meta>This page continues the mathematical defense of the number 25 as the square root of 666. The author is responding to a potential objection: since the square root of 666 (approximately 25.8) is numerically closer to 26 than 25, why not use 26? The catchword "non" from the previous page likely begins the phrase "not for that reason" or a similar negation in the closing sentence of the previous section.</meta>

### THE NUMBER 666.

How many feet long is the side of a square whose Area is exactly 666 square feet? A first person might perhaps respond that it is 25 feet long; because, clearly, this is the largest number of [whole] feet contained within it. A second might say it is 26 feet long: because the exact length comes closer to this number of feet. A third might respond that it is neither 25 feet nor 26 feet long, but that it is about 9 or 10 digits <note>original: "digitis." A digit was a historical unit of measure, approximately 1/16th of a foot or the width of a finger.</note> longer than 25 feet, and two or three digits shorter than 26. 

Of these three responses, it is manifest that the first is entirely true; since 25 is both the maximum and the minimum—and indeed the only—number of [whole] feet by which that length should be expressed. The second is manifestly false; for even if that length (which is at least two digits short of 26 feet) were short by only one digit, or even by some tiny fraction of one, it could not truly be said to be 26 feet. And whoever, in this case, takes that which is "closest to the truth" for the truth itself, it is certain that he takes a falsehood for the truth. The third response is <term>irrelevant to the point</term> <note>original: "ἀπροσδιόνυσον" (aprosdionyson). A Greek proverbial expression meaning "nothing to do with Dionysus," used to describe something off-topic or impertinent.</note>, and does not pertain to the matter proposed: for the question asked was not how many *digits* long the side of the figure was, but how many *feet* long.

<page-num>83</page-num>
<header>OF THE NUMBER 666.</header>

For the wall of the New Jerusalem is said to have twelve foundations; not because the number 12—whether regarding the Apostles themselves or the Christian Bishops (who are their legitimate successors in authority insofar as that authority was necessary for the perpetual government of the Church <term>Ecclefiæ Regimen</term>)—was intended to be the exact number of men for all future times, or even the most notable number within the Church. Rather, it was so that from this number, which serves as the **Root** <term>Radix</term> <note>In the mathematical and symbolic logic of the 17th century, the "root" is the foundational number. Here, the author alludes to 12 being the root of 144, the measurement of the New Jerusalem's wall in the Book of Revelation.</note> and **Basis** of another number, there might be a clear and powerful allusion. This allusion refers not just to the number itself, but to the nature, quality, and office of those from whom, as from a **Root**, the Hierarchy of the Church originally proceeded, upon whom it was built as its foundations, and in whom it was first clearly recognized.

Therefore, just as the number 12 is not applied to the Church Hierarchy <note>original: *Hierarchia*. From the Greek for "sacred rule," referring to the structured order of the clergy.</note> because of some permanent and unchanging number of men that must be perpetually maintained, so too the number 25 was not to be applied to the Roman Hierarchy in that same way. Instead, its true and accurate application was to be directed primarily toward this: that we might recognize not only the number, but also the Nature, Quality, and Office of those men from whom this **Pseudo-hierarchy** <note>original: *Pfeudo-hierarchia*. The author uses this term to label the Roman Catholic structure as a "false" or "counterfeit" sacred order.</note> derived its origin, upon whom it was as if "super-built," and in whom and among whom it was first clearly recognized.

<page-num>84</page-num>
<header>INTERPRETATION</header>

Thus, even though it might seem necessary at first glance that the number 25 should be a unique, constant, fixed, and perpetual number of Cardinals (or "Apostles of the Pope") to be applied to them throughout all the times the Antichrist shall endure, if the matter is weighed correctly, one must grant that such an application is not required. This is proven neither by the example of the opposite number <note>The "opposite number" refers to the 12 Apostles of Christ, discussed on the previous page.</note> (which applied only to the first number of the Apostles), nor does it fit the nature of a "type" <note>A "type" is a person, thing, or number in the Bible that serves as a symbol or foreshadowing of something else.</note>. For a symbolic type does not merely suggest a certain number of individuals, but rather suggests that those units are the **Root** <term>Radix</term> and **Basis** of others that are to spring from them and be built upon them.

Therefore, I say it must be fully granted that it is neither necessary nor likely that this number should be applied to them in any way other than by the count of those first instituted and established. That original number was the most conspicuous and notable, and was exactly applicable to them in the first foundation of their College <note>original: *Collegii*. Referring to the College of Cardinals.</note> and the first visible and actual institution of their order. Not only can the number of the **10 Crowned Horns** mentioned in the Apocalypse <note>The Book of Revelation.</note> be explained in the same way—in respect only to their first origin—but also the **Number of the Beast** <note>The famous 666.</note> is specifically to be referred back to the first original stock and **Image** of the Anti-Apostles <note>A term the author uses to describe the Cardinals as the inverse or "mirror" of Christ's Apostles.</note> of the Antichrist. ### C H A P T E R XVII.

*Isodorus Mosconius* <note>A 17th-century canon lawyer.</note> testifies to the same in these words: *Following this, Marcellus in the year 305, established by Decree that only 25 Titles were given, in which Baptism might be administered.*

# THE NUMBER 666.

...titles, 25 in number, as if they were Dioceses <note>The author uses the term "Dioceses" here in its older sense of administrative districts within the city, rather than a modern bishop’s territory.</note>, that is, Governments, established for the convenience of Baptism.

But above all others, *Onuphrio Panvinio* <note>original: "Onuphrius Panvinius" (1530–1568), an Augustinian friar and renowned historian of Roman antiquities.</note>, in his work *On the Principal Basilicas of the City of Rome*, describes these things most extensively of all; a brief summary of his words comes to this: Namely, since the number of Priests in Rome was originally small and uncertain, they were reduced to a fixed number and order by the Roman Popes Cletus and Evaristus. Cletus first reduced the Priests in Rome to the number of 25. Afterward, around the year of Christ 100, Evaristus designated and prescribed a distinct Parish <term>Parœcia</term> <note>From the Greek "paroikia," meaning a neighborhood or local church district, the origin of our word "parish."</note> for each Priest. These Parishes were later expanded and had their boundaries and limits more perfectly and accurately prescribed for them by Pope Dionysius, around the year of Christ 260. Afterward, however, Marcellus, around the year of Christ 305, limited the number of these Titles—which had long before been given to the Priests by Evaristus—and sanctioned by *Decree* that there should be 25 in Rome, as if they were so many Dioceses, for the more convenient Baptism of the Gentiles who were being converted daily to the Christian Religion. And this is the summary of what was said by Onuphrio concerning the original number of Cardinal Titles instituted at one and the same time and established by decree.

After the time of Marcellus, when the Church had been freed from persecutions, those Titles were increased by various Popes, as the same author testifies, with one Pope adding one and another adding another. Truly, as *St. Paul*...

### INTERPRETATION

Paul was not numbered among the 12 Apostles, because he was not from that number of 12 who were all chosen together at the same time and first called Apostles. In the same way, those Titles and Cardinals who were afterwards added one after another to those first 25 established by Decree and instituted at one and the same time, neither can nor should be counted among them. This is because the mystery consists (as has been clearly and manifestly proven above) in that number alone which truly belonged to them at the time when that order was first instituted and actually emerged.

And this primary number of 25 will further be proven by the testimony of *Girolamo Albani* <note>original: "Hieronymus Albanus" (1504–1591), an Italian cardinal and influential legal scholar.</note>, who mentions 25 Cardinals created by *Marcellus*.

The same is confirmed by the testimony of *Platina* <note>Bartolomeo Platina (1421–1481), a Renaissance humanist and author of the "Lives of the Popes."</note>, who writes to the same effect in these words: *Marcellus, devoting himself to divine worship, after he had urged the Roman Matron Priscilla to establish a Cemetery <note>The Catacombs of Priscilla on the Via Salaria.</note> at her own expense on the Salarian Way, established twenty-five Titles in the city of Rome, as if they were Dioceses, for the convenience of Baptism and the accessibility of those who daily came to the faith from the Gentiles.*

To the same purpose writes *Damasus* <note>Refers to the "Liber Pontificalis," which was historically attributed to Pope Damasus I.</note>, in these words: *Pope Marcellus established 25 Titles in Rome, as if they were Dioceses, on account of the Baptism and penance of the many who were being converted from the Pagans.*

The same is testified by *Anastasius* <note>Anastasius the Librarian (c. 810–878), another historian associated with the records of the Popes.</note>, who says of the same Pope Marcellus: *He established 25 Titles in the city of Rome, as if they were Dioceses.*

# INTERPRETATION

...gates be counted once, and seven of the old ones that have ceased to exist are passed over.

It appears from this discrepancy between the opinions of *Livy* and *Pliny* <note>Titus Livius and Pliny the Elder, whose accounts of Roman geography often varied due to the city's constant expansion.</note> (who lived not long after one another) that there were approximately 25 Gates in Rome at that time; for "more than one [thing] cannot be the truth." <note>original: *plus uno verum esse non potest*. A logical principle meaning that contradictory accounts cannot both be factual.</note> Nor is it likely that these authors were unaware of how many Gates were in Rome during their own lifetimes. But if there were 27 in *Livy's* time and no more than 24 in *Pliny's*, then it is probable that at some point during the intervening time there were 25.

But rather, it should be thought—just as in other great cities—that some gates are of greater and others of lesser note; some are public, while others belong rather to private houses or palaces; and some were of such doubtful location and use that their status is rightly debated, nor is it easy to say whether they should be called city gates or not. Thus, I say, it is likely that *Livy* counted two or three gates of lesser renown as city gates, which *Pliny* regarded as private. And perhaps some third person, tasked with stating the number of Rome's gates, would have included one of those three that *Pliny* omitted, and omitted two of those that *Livy* included; and perhaps he would have done so more correctly than either of them. For where authors, equal in credit and reputation, differ in opinion, the middle ground (if there be one) is more safely assumed than either of the extremes. <note>This "middle way" approach was a common scholarly method in the early modern period for resolving contradictions in classical texts.</note>

Indeed, I do not deny that this whole matter is nothing but

### THE NUMBER 666.

...unless it is a conjecture that the number of Rome's Gates at that time was 25.

I therefore allow *Onuphrius* <note>Onuphrio Panvinio (1530–1568), a renowned Augustinian friar and historian of Roman antiquities.</note>, that skilled writer of Roman Antiquities (who indeed, and as far as I know, is the only one to have written a specific Treatise on the Gates of Rome), to settle this dispute. It is certainly clear, from the words of *Onuphrius* just cited, that he claimed there were at least 24 gates in Rome in the time of Pliny; but it is also clear that among all of these, the *Triumphal Gate* was not counted. And so shortly thereafter, where he lists those 24 previously counted gates by name, he adds this one in the final place as a Gate of the City, although not as one of those previously counted; for he expressly adds, *The Triumphal Gate is beyond the number* <note>original: "Porta Triumphalis extra numerum"</note>. And when he later mentions two others, the *Window Gate of the Palace* and the *Dung Gate* <note>original: "Portam Fenestralem Palatii" and "Portam Stercorariam." These were specialized gates for the palace and for waste removal, respectively.</note>: regarding the former, which is the *Window Gate of the Palace*, he says, *I would believe it to have been not of the city, but rather of the Palace*; and of the other, which is the *Dung Gate*, he says, *not of the city, but of the Capitol*. Thus he explicitly excludes these last two from the count of City Gates; however, he explicitly adds the *Triumphal Gate* to that same count as one of the City Gates, just as other authors consider it, and even he himself (a) elsewhere, just as here, affirms that very thing in these words: *Part of the wall anciently circled through the middle of the Borgo, and it had two gates, the Aurelian and the Triumphal* <note>original: "Pars Muri antiquitus per medium Burgum gyrabat, & habebat duas Portas, Aureliam & Triumphalem." The "Borgo" refers to the area near the Vatican.</note>.

<meta>This page continues the author’s argument regarding the symbolic and historical significance of the number twenty-five in the topography of Rome. ### THE NUMBER 666.

Now, as for these 25 gates just enumerated, it is not our task to inquire how long they remained at that number, whether until the times of *Justinian* <note>Emperor Justinian I (r. 527–565 AD), who oversaw the restoration of Roman fortifications.</note> or even beyond. For just as those who affirm that there were 12 gates of Jerusalem do not mean that there were precisely that many at every point in time, but rather that there were that many when that city was at its most flourishing; or that there were that many, more or less, in common times—such that, all things considered, no other single number is more fitting for describing the Gates of Jerusalem than 12. The same must be said regarding the Gates of Rome and the number 25. For, just as with the Gates of Jerusalem, it is certain that the Gates of Rome, especially in later times, were repeatedly blocked up or altered; hence there is a great variety of opinions among authors, regarding both their number and their names.

But this should be noted: although the new addition to Rome, which is called the *Leonine City* <note>The *Civitas Leoniana*, the area around St. Peter's Basilica enclosed by walls by Pope Leo IV in the 9th century.</note>, brought with it 7 new gates; nevertheless, since some of the earlier ones have been abolished in various ways, it is possible that the same number of 25 still remains. This is expressly testified by *Severinus Binius* <note>A 17th-century Cologne historian and editor of the "General Councils."</note> in the first volume of the General Councils, page 261, where, either in his own words or those of *Georgius Braunius* <note>Author of the famous atlas *Theatrum Urbium* or *Civitates Orbis Terrarum* (1572).</note> in the *Theater of the Cities of the World*, he says: *Pliny reports there were 24 gates of the suburbs and the city; Livy, however, says 27. Now there are 365 towers; 25 gates remain.* <note>original Latin: "Portas Suburbiorum & Urbis 24 fuisse refert Plinius; Livius tamen 27. Nunc sunt Turres 365, Portæ 25 supersunt."</note>

And so I have shown that, however they are to be understood

*Just as 12,000 stadia are the solid measure <note>The author is referring to the cubic volume.</note> of a cube whose base perimeter equals the circumference of the New Jerusalem; so 25,000 stadia are the solid measure of a cube whose base perimeter equals the circumference of the City of Rome.*

The next step of application that follows must now be shown, concerning the measurement of the circumference or circuit of the City of Rome. This matter, through what has been shown before, is brought to this conclusion: namely, if the Pope is the Antichrist, and Rome is that city in which the Antichrist was especially to set up his kingdom; then the circumference or circuit of the Roman City would be a measure of, more or less, 116 or 117 **stadia** <term>Stadia: An ancient Greek unit of distance, roughly 600 feet. Eight stadia approximately equal one Roman mile.</term>; that is, 14$\frac{1}{2}$ **miles** <term>Milliaria: Roman miles, which are slightly shorter than modern statute miles.</term> and about $\frac{1}{8}$ of one mile. 

And indeed, this measurement fits so accurately, and is almost exactly the midpoint within that range of values which the varying opinions of writers allow concerning the circumference of this City after the Pope began to reign in it, that I could scarcely believe it possible for any other measurement to express it more truly.

In a matter so manifest, there is no need to bring forward the opinions of many authors individually; especially since there will be an opportunity to say more below, where I am about to speak <note>original: "dicturus"</note> concerning the Shape of the City and the Shape of the number 666.

However, where the passage of time and the differing opinions of men have introduced such a variety of measurements, I leave it to anyone to judge whether that measurement of nearly 14 5/8 miles does not compete in probability with any of those others, as it stands, so to speak, in the very middle of them.

I conclude, therefore: just as the solid measure of an imaginary cube...

<note>original: "Pomœrii"; The *pomerium* was the sacred, religious boundary of the city of Rome, which could only be expanded by leaders who had enlarged the empire's territory.</note>

Concerning the number of Churches, he writes thus: *“Moreover, why no more and no fewer Churches were established at the same time to be honored with these names, it will be worth explaining; and this is most fitting to our purpose, since this matter has been celebrated with a remarkable mystery.”* For although (<sup>a</sup>) Onuphrius <note>Onuphrio Panvinio (1529–1568) was an Augustinian friar and a renowned historian and antiquarian who wrote extensively on Roman history and the papacy.</note> uses these words regarding other churches in Rome as well, and not only regarding these 25, which were first called *Tituli* <term>The *tituli* (titles) were the earliest parish churches of Rome, each assigned to a specific cardinal-priest. They served as the administrative foundation for the Roman Church.</term>; nevertheless, his words can rightly apply only to these churches. This is because, in his own judgment and according to that "cloud of witnesses" <note>A biblical reference to Hebrews 12:1.</note> I mentioned above, there was never a complete group of churches in the City of Rome that were called *Tituli* and (as he says here) were established at the same time, except for this one group of the 25 churches mentioned above. If, therefore, there is any mystery inherent in such a number, it must be that it applies to this number 25, and to no other.

(a) Onuphrius (in *On the Principal Basilicas of the City of Rome*, chapter 2) testifies that the 25 *Tituli* were established all at once; and that this number was afterwards increased successively, with one Pope adding one and another adding another, until finally the number reached 28, which he conjectures happened around the time of Leo I <note>Leo the Great, Pope from 440 to 461 AD.</note>, in the year 440.

### Chapter XXIV.

*A brief and summary enumeration of some other things of lesser note to which the number 25 is conspicuously applied in the City and the Roman Church.*

It must now be said of many other things worthy of note, concerning the Papacy and the Roman Church, in which there is a notable fondness <note>original: "affectatio"; implies a deliberate striving for or artificial attachment to a particular form</note> for the number 25. These will, however, be spoken of briefly and in passing, because I consider them to be additional and incidental, not an essential part of this interpretation. Yet they are mostly such that they aptly correspond, to a certain extent, to those things to which the number 12 is applied throughout the Holy Scriptures.

Just as the land of Canaan was distributed into 12 jurisdictions and divisions, which were ruled by the heads of the Tribes; which perhaps was a type <term>In biblical interpretation, a "type" is a historical person or event that foreshadows a future reality.</term> of the distribution shown by Ezekiel (a), or of that ecclesiastical governance administered by the Apostles, not only as pastors in the city of Jerusalem, but also in the entire region which per- <note>The text breaks here; "per-" likely continues as "pertingebat" (extended/reached).</note>

### THE NUMBER 666

(ª) *Onuphrius* brings forward **St. Augustine** and **St. Jerome** as witnesses. This Altar, or tomb, is square on all sides, in a perfectly cubic form, having its length, width, and height equal to one another. The area of each side or face is precisely 25 square feet, as both (ᵇ) *Baronius* <note>Caesar Baronius (1538–1607) was an influential Cardinal and Vatican librarian known for his massive history of the Church, the *Annales Ecclesiastici*.</note> and *Onuphrius* clearly testify—at least to those who sufficiently understand what a square or superficial measurement is.

But the number 25 is also conspicuously and notably impressed upon all their Altars: because of the five wounds of Christ, as they say, which are engraved five times in different places on the top of the Altar, or the uppermost face <note>The author refers to the practice of carving five small crosses on the altar slab, representing the five wounds of Christ. By multiplying these five wounds across five locations, he arrives at the number 25.</note>. What this multiplication of the wounds of Christ, from 5 into 25, signifies—whether in their own intention or beyond it (whether because they themselves offer up Christ more often, whom the Jews crucified only once; or because their own Apostasy has inflicted more wounds upon Him than the cruelty of the Jews)—that is my...

---

<margin>(a) St. Jerome, in his work *Against Vigilantius*, testifies that the Roman Pontiff offers sacrifices to the Lord over the venerable bones of Peter and Paul, and considers their tombs to be the Altars of Christ. *St. Augustine, Letter 42, to the Madaurensians*: "The most eminent summit of the most noble Roman Empire, with his crown lowered, supplicates at the tomb of the Fisherman *Peter*." *Onuphrius, ibid.*</margin>

<margin>(b) Constantine built a Basilica for the blessed Apostle Peter in the Temple of Apollo. # INTERPRETATION

...it is not of great importance to investigate <note>The author is picking up from the previous page's thought regarding the specific theological motivations behind multiplying the five wounds of Christ by five.</note>. However, it is certain that it is a solemn and standard practice to imprint precisely 25 points, marks, small pits, or characters <term>characters<note>original: *characteres*. In this context, it refers to engraved signs or symbols rather than letters.</note></term> upon all their altars. To better understand the square measurements of the Altar—or Tomb—of Saint Peter mentioned earlier, as well as the method of arranging those 25 marks on their altars, I have included this illustration of the Altar for your consideration.

->*Figure of the Altar of Saint Peter, in the Basilica of Saint Peter in Rome.*<-

<image-desc>A perspective woodcut illustration of a cubic altar or tomb structure resting on a rectangular base. The top surface of the altar is divided into a 5x5 grid of 25 squares. Small dots or "puncta" are marked at the intersections and corners of the grid. The number "5" is printed along the edges to indicate the dimensions, and the number "25" is visible on the top surface and the front face, representing the total count of the squares/marks.</image-desc>

### THE NUMBER 666

The Feast of St. Bartholomew is celebrated in Rome on the 25th day of August, as their Breviary <note>A liturgical book containing the public prayers, hymns, and readings of the Roman Catholic Church.</note> testifies, even though in other places it is celebrated one day earlier <note>Most of the Western world celebrates St. Bartholomew on August 24.</note>. And this singular fact seems to clearly testify to their peculiar affection for the 25th day: indeed, since all those other Feasts are celebrated on the 25th day of their respective months everywhere by the authority of the Roman Church; yet they wished this very thing to be unique to the city of Rome, that they would celebrate one additional 25th day more than the rest of the world does.

And this point is worth observing: that *Antiochus* <note>Antiochus IV Epiphanes, the Seleucid king (reigned 175–164 BCE) whose persecution of the Jews led to the Maccabean Revolt.</note>, who in almost all respects was a Type <note>In biblical interpretation, a "Type" is a historical person or event seen as a foreshadowing of a later entity—in this case, the Antichrist.</note> of the Antichrist, did not deviate even in this regard. ### Chapter 27.

*Answering certain doubts concerning the fractions of the root of the number 666. The root of the number 666 is more accurately applicable to the Papacy than the root of any perfect square would be.*

Since I now believe I have sufficiently proven all that I promised regarding the application of the number 25 to the Papacy—both in terms of those things essential to this application, and also in terms of certain other things which may have been added by chance—I now proceed to address certain objections that might seem to oppose what has been said. I will show that, although some things might seem objectable to this interpretation, nevertheless, when they are duly weighed and properly considered, they confirm it even more fully. Indeed, they open the way to mysteries concerning the Papacy that are no less—if not even greater—than those which have been declared so far.

<meta>catchword: can</meta>

Insofar as it is an absolute number in itself, and insofar as it is merely a **discrete quantity** <term>discrete quantity: a mathematical term for something that consists of separate units (like whole numbers), as opposed to continuous quantity which can be infinitely divided</term>, for that reason its **Root** cannot be called something in the middle between 25 and 26; neither as a middle of *Participation* nor as a middle of *Abnegation* <note>The author uses Scholastic logic here: a "middle of participation" shares qualities of both extremes, while a "middle of abnegation" is something that is neither of the extremes.</note>. It is not a middle of *Abnegation*; because from different perspectives it can be either, and indeed from different perspectives (though not with the same precise language) it can be both. It is not a middle of *Participation*; because, in absolute numbers, the **Units** follow one another immediately, and do not allow any breadth, whether of extension or of some intermediate name; for **Units** in absolute numbers are simply and absolutely (a) indivisible, whether into parts of the same kind or parts of another kind. Therefore, just as it is absurd and impossible to say that the number of **Cardinals** at their first institution was 25 Cardinals and a half, or 25 and three-quarters of a Cardinal; so it is similarly inconsistent and impossible to say that the **Root** of the number 666 (when considered in its pure essentials as an absolute number) is 25 **Units** and a half, or 25 and three-quarters of a **Unit**. But just as the **Root** of that number is properly and expressly 25, yet in some respect 26; so the number of **Cardinals** is properly and expressly 25, as has been proven above; yet when the **Pope** is numbered among them, it can be said in some respect

---

# THE NUMBER 666.

If, therefore, a reason is further required as to why the number 666 was chosen for the number of the Beast rather than any other number that has 25 as its **Root** <term>Radix: The square root. While the square root of 666 is approximately 25.8, the author uses 25 as the functional integer root for his symbolic calculations.</term>: I say that for whatever reason the number 144 was chosen over any other that has 12 as its **Root**, for that very same reason was the number 666 chosen over any other that has 25 as its **Root**. Now, in order that this may be more correctly understood, I will briefly explain what is meant by a **Figured number**. The sum of those things handed down by **Euclid** and others *concerning plane figured numbers* amounts roughly to this: that "plane figured numbers" are those which are made or produced either by multiplying a number by itself, or at least by multiplying one number by another. For example, the number 12 is a figured number, as one that is made or produced by multiplying the number 3 by 4, or even by multiplying 2 by 6, as is clear from the figures joined below, where 12 units, perpendicular— <note>original: "normali-". Among all four-sided figures that have the <term>same perimeter<note>original: "isoperimetris"; having the same total boundary length. Therefore, when the **Figure** of a given number is sought, that specific Figure of the number is primarily chosen which is either a perfect square or comes closest to it. This is done all the more because the imaginary measurements of the New Jerusalem—by which the true measurements of the literal Jerusalem are truly, yet mystically, designated—depend as much on the Shape of the city described there as on the **solid number** <note>A "solid number" in 17th-century mathematics is a number that represents a three-dimensional figure, such as a cube, formed by multiplying three factors.</note> mentioned in that same place.

It may be objected that the imaginary solid Shape, by which the **Perimeter** <note>The total distance around the boundary of the city.</note> of the New Jerusalem was found, was created by multiplying the whole **Area** by either its length or its width; and therefore, that imaginary solid Shape by which the Perimeter of the opposing city [Rome] is to be found ought not to be a **cube**, as was stated above, but should instead be formed solely from the multiplication of the Area by its length or width, without any regard for its Shape.

Indeed, it is possible that the literal Jerusalem was not precisely and exactly a square Shape with equal sides; yet its circuit was equal to the circuit of the Base of the cube mentioned in the description of the New Jerusalem.

> (a) This perimeter is indeed a little more than 140 stadia, that is, a little more than 17$\frac{1}{2}$ miles <term>milliaria: Roman miles, each consisting of 1,000 paces.</term>. # NUMBER 666.

...through the Root and Figure of this number 666, has so exquisitely described and shown beforehand all the notable differences in the sizes of the city of Rome, from the time the Pope began to reign there, that no one could reasonably demand anything more accurate.

But because these matters are obscure and intricate, and those who do not possess (or perhaps even those who do) a moderate skill in Geometry cannot easily find the Perimeter of the Base of a solid Figure <note>A three-dimensional geometric object, such as a cube or rectangular prism.</note> when given only the solid capacity <note>The volume of the shape.</note> and the proportion of the sides to each other, without any linear measurements being provided: Therefore, to assist those who desire these things to be made clear and obvious to the senses, I would like them to consider this certain Rule: *as weight is to weight, so the solid is to the solid.* <note>original: "ut pondus ponderi, ita solidum solido." This refers to the principle that objects of the same material have volumes proportional to their weights.</note>

If, therefore, one ounce of soft wax, clay, or any homogeneous soft body is taken and formed into a Cubic shape, so that its length, width, and height are equal to one another: let it be understood to represent 1,000 solid <term>stadia: an ancient unit of distance, roughly 600 feet; here used as a unit of volume (cubic stadia).</term>. Then, by dividing one side of this tiny cube into 10 equal parts, each of these will represent the true length of one stadium. (For indeed, 1,000 is a perfectly cubic number, whose cubic root is 10.) Then let a measuring scale, or a divided line, be made, which has parts equal to those, with the number 100 or 200 marked upon it.

# INTERPRETATION

...firstly, that the reading I have followed is the one more generally and commonly accepted. Secondly, that those very words "twelve thousand [and] twelve" <note>original Greek: δώδεκα χιλιάδων δώδεκα</note> (if they were to be read in that way) more properly and much more likely signify *twelve thousand and twelve*—which introduces very little difference in the measurements—than *twelve times twelve thousand*. Indeed, if this latter number were intended, it would have to be said "twelve times twelve thousand" <note>original Greek: δώδεκα χιλιάδων δωδεκάκις</note>, or rather "twelve times twelve thousand" <note>original Greek: δωδεκάκις δώδεκα χιλιάδων</note>, or "one hundred forty-four thousand" <note>original Greek: ἑκατὸν τεσσαράκοντα τεσάρων χιλιάδων</note>. Finally, even if both readings were equally probable, the measurement of 12,000 <term>stadia: an ancient unit of length, roughly 600 feet; the author uses it here to define the city's dimensions.</term> matches the measurements of the same city described by <term>Ezekiel: an Old Testament prophet who provided highly detailed architectural measurements of a visionary holy city and temple.</term>, while the alternative reading does not match them. Therefore, the former should be considered the true reading, not the latter. For how might we better interpret a doubtful or ambiguous passage of Holy Scripture than by comparing it with another passage of Scripture that is clear and established beyond dispute?

If it is further objected that 12,000 stadia cannot be the exact measurement of the New Jerusalem—since the figure of the New Jerusalem is exactly <term>cubic: a three-dimensional shape with equal height, width, and depth. The author is noting that 12,000 is not a "perfect cube" (a number resulting from an integer multiplied by itself twice).</term>, but the number 12,000 is not exactly cubic—I respond: although some authors who have written about these measurements seem to suggest an objection of this kind regarding the solid measurement; and <term>Lyranus: Nicholas of Lyra (c. 1270–1349), an influential Franciscan scholar whose literal biblical commentaries were standard for centuries.</term> suggests it regarding the square measurement of the city's area when he tries to apply this number 12,000 to it; nevertheless, there is no weight in this objection.

# THE NUMBER 666.

...objection. And so, I humbly and earnestly request that pious, prudent, and learned men—who are not yet convinced that the <term>Pope: the Bishop of Rome, whom the author, writing from a Protestant perspective, identifies as the Antichrist.</term> is the Antichrist, nor that this is the true interpretation of this number—would not consider their own opposing views to be so <term>apodictic: clearly established or beyond dispute.</term> as if nothing but vain bickering could be offered in response to them. ### THE TRUMPET BLAST OF FRANCESCO BROCCARDO, *who had been Secretary to Pope Clement VIII; which—having been converted to the sincere Faith of Christ—he sounded to all the Kings of his time who favor the Evangelical Religion, against the Pope and the Papal Machinations and Tricks revealed by him.*

Since the most kind God has wonderfully raised me up from the thickest darkness of the <term>Papacy: the office and jurisdiction of the Pope, here used to describe the Roman Catholic system.</term> to this clearest light of His <term>Gospel: literally "good news," referring here to the Protestant or "Evangelical" understanding of the Bible.</term>, and to a purer knowledge of His word, I could rightly and deservedly be considered the most ungrateful of all living men if I did not dedicate and devote what remains of my life to the glory of His most holy name and to the benefit of the Evangelical kingdom. # THE TRUMPET-CALL OF FRANCESCO BROCCARDO, *who had been Secretary to Pope Clement VIII; which, having converted to the sincere Faith of Christ, he sounded to all the Kings of his time who favor the Evangelical Religion, against the Pope and the Papal Plots and Schemes revealed by him.*

### A Battle Signal against the Pope ###

...the words of the cardinals, and I have gathered and learned it from the writings, signatures, and seals of the Popish <term>Popish: A term used by Protestant writers to refer to Roman Catholics (Pontificii in Latin).</term> Princes. Let them be indignant, let them snarl, and (if God permits them) let them rush upon me: I feel and acknowledge that I am driven and moved by God to this task; I will not stand with them, and while they rage, I will intrepidly expose their treachery. Therefore, let as many as sincerely favor the Gospel hear, and let them keep what they have heard in their hearts.

Clement VIII <note>Clement VIII was Pope from 1592 to 1605, a period of intense religious conflict in Europe.</note>, the Roman Pontiff who currently occupies the seat, has bound the minds of nearly all the Princes of Europe to himself with a certain marvelous cunning. # THE WRITINGS OF FRANCESCO BROCCARDO #

...almost all other Popish Princes <term>Popish Princes: Broccardo’s term for Catholic monarchs loyal to the Pope.</term>, except for the King of France and the Grand Duke of Tuscany <note>original: "Dux Hetruriæ"</note>, have given their names and subscribed to the said treaty. The Pope is leaving no stone unturned <note>original: "movet omnem lapidem," a Latin idiom meaning to use every possible means.</note> to urge others also to join with them, and it seemed he had managed to create some hope of success.

2. There are many chapters in this treaty: specifically concerning the method of action, the timing, the preparations, and the portion which each member is to contribute to that **holy war** which they are meditating and plotting against the **Evangelicals** <term>Evangelicals: At this time, the term (Euangelici) referred generally to Protestants who claimed to follow the "evangelium" or Gospel.</term>. All these chapters converge upon a single point: that all the aforementioned allies, with joined forces, strive for the extirpation of the Evangelical Religion by whatever means they can.

3. A year ago, the Emperor <note>Likely Holy Roman Emperor Rudolf II (reigned 1576–1612).</note> was pressing the Pope for money and aid against the Turk <note>The "Turk" refers to the Ottoman Empire, which was then a major military threat to the eastern borders of the Holy Roman Empire.</note>, so that he might either repress their attacks or at least force them into honorable terms of peace or a long-lasting truce. He promised that once matters with the Turk were settled according to his wish, he would turn all his strength and forces toward the service of the Pope against the Evangelicals, whom they themselves call **Heretics**.

4. The Pope strongly favors peace with the Turks, and for this reason, he refused last year to hear the ambassadors of the King of the Persians, who were urging a general confederation against the Turk. For the Pope judges that the Evangelicals are more detrimental to his tyranny than the Turk is. He is also often accustomed to complain that for forty whole years, war has been waged without fruit...

-> **A Battle Cry against the Pope** <-

[...it would be use]less to fight against the Turks at a time when, with less expense, the Roman Church could have recovered its authority in Europe. For this reason, he <note>Referring to Pope Clement VIII.</note> desires to lead the Turk to seek peace, and advises the Emperor to accept fair conditions of peace from the Turks.

5. In the year 1601, Cardinal von Dietrichstein <note>Franz von Dietrichstein (1570–1636), a powerful prince-bishop and a leading figure of the Counter-Reformation.</note>, the Bishop of Olomouc <note>original: "Epiſcopus Olomutienſis"</note>, traveled from Prague to Rome with extreme haste. He carried letters from the Emperor to the Pope, in which the Emperor promised the Pope the military enforcement <note>original: "executionem armatam"</note> of the sentence he had previously issued in favor of the Pope against the **Evangelicals**. This concerned the matter of Church property, which the Emperor wanted restored in all places for the use of the Roman Church according to the directives of the legates. From this, the Pope hoped to find an opportunity to stir up troubles and unrest in **Germany**; for this reason, they agreed that the greater part of the said property should be distributed to those who fought vigorously in that war.

6. At the same time, this same Cardinal, in the name of the Emperor (as he himself boasted), promised that—while the necessary preparations for open war were being made—the Emperor and the Austrian Princes would completely expel all **Evangelicals** from their ancestral lands <term>patrimonial lands: The "Länder" or territories held by the House of Habsburg by right of inheritance.</term>, namely from **Austria**, **Tyrol**, **Croatia**, **Carinthia**, **Styria**, and similar places. Furthermore, they intended to attack them in **Moravia**, **Silesia**, and **Bohemia**—first without an outcry, if it could be done, and then by arms and force, especially using foreign—

Because of this, the Papal Legate remarked that many German nobles are daily renouncing **Lutheranism** and returning to the Roman Catholic religion...

### The Battle-Cry against the Pope <note>original: "Classicum adversus Papam"</note>
<sig>N 3</sig>
<meta>catchword: fert</meta>

...a smaller number of **Evangelical** <note>Protestant reformers</note> soldiers, lest they should become experienced to the ruin of the **Roman Church**, and lest they become superior to the **Catholics**. It would be better if the **Emperor** <note>The Holy Roman Emperor</note> recruited only **Papal** <note>original: "Pontificios"</note> soldiers and exacted wages and funds from the **Protestants**; thus, in a short time, the **Evangelicals** would be both disarmed and impoverished at once. Many things that have been discussed could be begun and attempted for the destruction of the **Evangelicals** which, as things currently stand, could not even be contemplated. And so that the **Emperor** might be led to this, he accused the **Evangelicals** of an alliance with the **Turk** <note>The Ottoman Empire, often used in political rhetoric to frame Protestants as traitors to Christendom.</note>.

13. The **Pope** <note>original: "Papa"</note> has certain Councilors in the court of the **Emperor** <note>original: "Cæsaris"</note> most devoted to him, whom he sustains with great salaries and high hopes; they bend the **Emperor** to the **Pope’s** will, and through their work, he learns not only the **Emperor's** actions but even his inclinations and feelings.

14. The **Pope** urges the election of a **King of the Romans** <note>The title given to the heir-apparent who would succeed the Holy Roman Emperor.</note>. For he fears lest, upon the **Emperor's** death with no successor substituted for him, someone might be elected who is not a **Papist**, or at least one who is not hostile to the **Evangelicals**; and for this reason, he incites the **Emperor** and earnestly recommends **Albert** <note>Archduke Albert VII of Austria, a staunch Catholic leader.</note> to him and the **Ecclesiastical Electors** <note>The high-ranking bishops who held votes in the imperial election.</note>. He desires **Albert** to be preferred over others for many names <note>reasons</note>, but especially because he believes that through him, the **Roman Religion** must be restored in the **Low Countries** <note>original: "Belgio"</note> and **Germany**, and also because the **King of Spain** offers—

16. For the **Pope** and the clergy do not look so much at the cause of Religion as at political reasons: They want **Albert**, not because he is more religious, but because they believe he will be more powerful and will more effectively diminish the **Evangelical Princes**.

17. Because of this election, many measures against the **Evangelicals** are being delayed, which would be carried out immediately if the election fell to another **Austrian** prince. For the **Emperor** fears that if he should behave with open hostility toward the **Evangelicals** before the aforementioned successor is appointed, the Princes would be alienated from him and his family...

21. For they hope that by **Jesuitical arts** <note>a contemporary term for the strategic and educational methods used by the Society of Jesus</note> so much can be achieved that they will have many supporters in those cities—which are otherwise **Evangelical** <note>Protestant</note>—by whose help they hope the **Evangelicals** can be more easily oppressed. The question then was only regarding their maintenance. For the **Bishops of Germany** were complaining about the thinness of their revenues; but the **Pope** finally decided to maintain them at the expense of the **ecclesiastical treasury** <note>original: "fisci Ecclesiastici"</note>, until they were otherwise provided for.

22. **Archduke Ferdinand**, the **Prince of Styria and Carinthia** <note>Ferdinand II (1578–1637), a staunch advocate of the Counter-Reformation who later became Holy Roman Emperor</note>, recently complained to the **Pope** <note>original: "Pontifice"</note>. He lamented that although he had personally expelled all **Evangelicals** <note>original: "Euangelicos"; a term used here for Protestants</note> from his own provinces, many still lived there due to the leniency of certain **Ecclesiastical Princes** <note>Bishops who ruled their own territories as secular lords</note>. These bishops—specifically the **Bishop of Bamberg** and the **Archbishop of Salzburg**—held certain lands within those provinces and were continuing to tolerate and welcome the Protestants. Consequently, Ferdinand asked the **Pope** to sternly rebuke those bishops and force them to completely expel the aforementioned people. Otherwise, Ferdinand threatened that he would exercise his own supreme authority over those lands and drive them out by force of arms, even against the will of the bishops—a plan which greatly pleased the **Pope**.

23. Meanwhile, as preparations for open war against the **Evangelicals** are underway, these **Papists** <note>original: "Pontificii"; those loyal to the Pope</note> are striving to attack the **Gospel**. This year, they sent a massive number of **deceivers and spies** <note>original: "seductorum & exploratorum"</note> to parts of **Belgium** and **England**. Through their efforts, many people of all ranks are being led astray and bribed; these individuals are then supported in **Protestant cities** at the **Pope’s** expense, so they may be useful for the schemes he is brewing. A month ago, some of these **emissaries** passed through **Leipzig** <note>original: "Lipsia"</note> in disguise, traveling toward **Prussia**, where they hope to "build a nest" <note>original: "nidificare"; meaning to establish a permanent base of operations</note>, as will be discussed below. If the actions of such spies were observed more diligently, several could easily be captured, and from their logs and notes, these and many more plots would be uncovered.

-># THE WORKS OF FRANCIS BROCCARD<-
<page-num>202</page-num>
<header>THE WORKS OF FRANCIS BROCCARD</header>

24. A few years ago, this **Pope** <note>Referring to the reigning Pope, likely Clement VIII (r. 1592–1605)</note> established two congregations or senates in Rome. One is called the ***Congregation for the Propagation of the Faith*** <note>original: "Congregatio ad propagationem Fidei"; an organization created to spread Catholicism in non-Catholic lands</note>; the other is the ***Congregation of the Sacred League*** <note>original: "Congregatio sacri foederis"</note>. In each of these, there are nine **Cardinals** and an equal number of Counselors. They meet together once every week and do nothing, meditate on nothing, and study nothing except how to devise, propose, and examine methods for spreading the **Pope’s tyranny** and for paving the way for persecutions and slaughterhouses against the **Evangelicals** <note>Protestants</note>. They also hunt for any opportunity to ignite that cursed war against the faithful. From this congregation come those **Emissaries and spies** <note>original: "Emissarii illi & exploratores"</note> who are sent out every year in great numbers to disturb the Evangelical provinces and to lead the faithful astray. In short, these two congregations are the workshops in which all the instruments are shaped—the machines, betrayals, plots, crimes, and all manner of evils against the Evangelicals are manufactured there. Moreover, these aforementioned congregations maintain special communication with the **Jesuits** and the students of the **German, English, and Moravian colleges**. From these institutions, as if from a **Trojan horse**, traitors and deceivers emerge, whom they send—as I have said—to all the Evangelical provinces. Indeed, these men are judged to be the most fit of all for trickery and betrayals. For because of the languages they master, and because of the customs, family connections, and networks of clients they possess in all the Evangelical provinces, they easily—

33. They were already boasting that they had established something certain, and had drawn those **Counts** toward the **Spanish** party and the **Roman Religion**. They maintained several **Jesuits** among them to instruct them and keep them in their duty and devotion to the **Roman See**. But when those matters turned out poorly due to the vigilance of the **States**, they are now contemplating other remedies.

<sig>O 2</sig>

Furthermore, who is so stupid and devoid of sense as to not know what mind the **Roman Pontiffs** have always had, and still have, toward the Empire? They have always been rivals and declared enemies of its scope and majesty. Therefore, even to the **Papal Princes** themselves, these counsels ought to be suspect when the "good of the Empire" is at stake.

37. From the same source—or rather the abyss—of the aforementioned confederation against the **Evangelicals** <note>Protestants</note>, flowed those wars and movements attempted this year against the people of **Geneva** and **Strasbourg** <note>original: "Argentoratenses"</note>. These were like preludes to that general persecution which they are contemplating. Through these movements, they tested the resolve and the strength of the Evangelicals; they observed whether they would join their arms to defend the common cause, or whether each one would protect his own affairs separately. I had forewarned fifteen months ago, through my letters and through faithful friends, both the Genevans and the Strasbourgers about the plan held among the Papal confederates...

38. It was not without a most crafty plan that they had resolved to attack **Geneva** and the people of **Strasbourg** <note>original: "Argentoratenses"</note> at the same time. For by stirring up these two conflicts simultaneously, they hoped to so deter and occupy the **Evangelical Swiss** <note>the Protestant Cantons</note> with the Genevan war that they would be unable to provide aid to the people of Strasbourg, as they had done nine years earlier. Furthermore, they judged that once the war in Strasbourg had begun, the **Protestant Princes** would be so anxious and fearful that they would not allow any soldiers from their territories to go to the aid of the **States of Holland** <note>original: "Ordinibus Bataviæ"</note> against **Albert** <note>Archduke Albert of Austria, the Habsburg governor of the Spanish Netherlands</note>. ### A Clarion Call against the Pope
<page-num>213</page-num>
->O 3<-

...of the Provinces, a place for the seditious and turbulent where they may exercise and satisfy their fury and bloodthirsty spirit. This strategy has at all times been the seedbed and fuel for many wars throughout the entire world. The **Italian Popes** and the **Spanish Kings** have long used this, whereby they have remained at peace in their own homes for many years now, as spectators of the wars in **Belgium**, **France**, and **Hungary**—wars which they themselves have incited, inflamed, and even now foster and nourish for the aforementioned purpose. 

Those most noble **Germans** of past centuries did not wait for anyone to meet them at home or attack them in their fatherland, but rather they marched out to meet them all the way into **Italy**, **France**, and **Spain**. That heroic virtue and those divine impulses, by which they tamed nearly the whole world with arms, are not yet extinguished in the souls of the Germans; let them but wake up, and exercise those most brave hearts and invincible strength against those effeminate and cowardly petty-priests <note>original: "sacrificulos"; a derogatory term for Catholic priests, implying they are mere "performers of sacrifices" rather than true ministers</note> who, although they now act ferociously and insult us, relying on our laziness, will nevertheless not dare to wait for the Germans in Italy—for they could not even look upon their faces in former times without trembling. 

Let the **Germans** read the deeds of their ancestors, celebrated by the writers of every age, and let them learn at last to be Germans—that is, the **oppressors of Tyrants**, the **champions of Liberty**, and the **masters of the World**—nor should they allow the **hooded Pope** <note>original: "Papam Bardocucullum"; *bardocucullus* refers to a coarse hooded cloak worn by peasants or monks, used here by Broccardus as a mocking epithet for the Pope</note> and certain cowardly and idle **Papist Princes** to be so petulan-

<meta>catchword: lan- (petulantiam)</meta>

<summary>Broccardus argues that the Papacy and Spanish Monarchy preserve peace in their own lands by inciting wars in Protestant territories, and he calls upon the German people to reclaim their ancestral martial spirit to overthrow these oppressors.</summary>

<keywords>German nationalism, anti-Catholic polemic, Papacy, Spanish Empire, military virtue, religious wars, Thirty Years' War context</keywords>

### THE WORKS OF FRANCISCO BROCCARDO
<page-num>214</page-num>

...insolently to insult the fierce and manly German people.

39. There was a persistent rumor—one which the **French** themselves boasted of—that **Geneva** had been attacked by the **Duke of Savoy** <note>Charles Emmanuel I, who famously attempted to seize the Protestant stronghold of Geneva in the "Escalade" of 1602</note> with the knowledge and permission of the **King of France** <note>Henry IV of France</note>. For, according to an agreement made two years prior by **Cardinal Aldobrandini** <note>Pietro Aldobrandini, a powerful papal diplomat and nephew of Pope Clement VIII</note> between the French King and the Duke of Savoy, it was agreed and settled that the Duke of Savoy could attack Geneva without offending the King and without breaking the peace. This was on the condition, however, that once captured, the city would either be demolished or divided in such a way that the part across the **Rhone** toward France would belong to the King, while the part on this side of the Rhone toward the **Savoyards** <note>original: "Allobrogos"; referring to the people of the Savoy region</note> would belong to the Duke. 

At that meeting with Aldobrandini, the King of France made many promises regarding the acceptance of the **Council of Trent** <note>The landmark Catholic council that redefined doctrine in opposition to the Reformation</note>, the aforementioned alliance, and the restoration of the **Jesuits**. Although these matters have been delayed until now, **Cardinal d'Ossat** <note>original: "Dosutus"; Arnaud d'Ossat, a French diplomat and cardinal</note> and the King’s ambassador assured the **Pope** <note>Clement VIII</note> that the King would fulfill everything in due time. They testified that he was most favorably inclined toward the **Roman See**, but that he had been unable to keep his promises thus far because of the treaty he had to confirm with the **Swiss** and several **Protestant Princes** <note>original: "Principes Evangelicos"</note>. He feared offending them by carrying out the aforementioned actions, yet he would perform them shortly. But to tell the truth, I hold the same opinion regarding the King of France that I have often heard from the mouth of this Pope—namely, that the King...

<meta>catchword: gem (This likely leads into "geminus" or "gemere" on the following page.)</meta>

<summary>Broccardus details a secret conspiracy between France, Savoy, and the Papacy to dismantle the Protestant stronghold of Geneva and restore Catholic influence through the Jesuits and the Council of Trent.</summary>

## A BATTLE-CRY AGAINST THE POPE
<page-num>215</page-num>
<sig>O 4</sig>
<meta>catchword: con-</meta>

...[the King] was crafty and devoted to neither party.

40. The **King of Spain** <note>Philip III of Spain</note>, under the pretext of the siege of **Algiers** in Africa, has equipped a maritime fleet in the **Mediterranean** and **Tyrrhenian** seas, which will nevertheless depart through the **Strait of Hercules** <note>original: "fretum Herculeum"; the Strait of Gibraltar</note>, whether against the **Dutch** <note>original: "Hollandos"</note> or against the **English** <note>original: "Anglos"</note>. It is in expectation of this fleet that **Albert** <note>Archduke Albert VII of Austria, then sovereign of the Spanish Netherlands</note> has delayed for so long at **Ostend** <note>A strategic port city in Flanders that was the site of a brutal three-year siege from 1601 to 1604</note>. 

There are many arguments proving this intention of his, but one is quite apparent: that this fleet is equipped for the most part with very large ships suited to the waves of the **Ocean**, and little suited to the shores of Africa and the Mediterranean, for which **Triremes** or **Galleys** <note>Long, shallow-draft vessels powered by oars, common in the calmer Mediterranean</note> are more fit—and **Charles V** <note>The Holy Roman Emperor (reigned 1519–1556) and grandfather of the current Spanish King</note> used only those. However, there are other more certain arguments, which I myself have observed from their secret councils. 

Therefore, let those whom it concerns take heed! They will not long delay this breakout, provided they can promise themselves some safe harbor in the stretch of the **Ocean** sea, which they are striving for with all their strength. For they say that only this one thing stands in the way of their success: that they have no ports or channels capable of holding a numerous fleet. If they are eventually able to occupy such a port, they boast that they will possess innumerable and insuperable forces by sea and land. And indeed, there is a very great number in **Holland**, **Zeeland**, **Friesland**, **England**, and **Scotland** of those who follow the factions of the **Papists** <note>original: "Pontificiorum"</note> and the **Spaniards**, and who urge the Papal Princes to take up arms...

<summary>Broccardo warns that the Spanish naval buildup in the Mediterranean is a ruse for an invasion of Northern Europe, noting that their large ships are designed for the Atlantic rather than the African coast. He claims that Spanish success depends only on securing a deep-water port and mobilizing Catholic sympathizers already living in Protestant lands.</summary>

<keywords>Spanish Navy, Philip III, Siege of Ostend, Archduke Albert, Naval Warfare, Catholic Faction, Holland, England, Scotland, Geopolitics</keywords>

<header>FRANCIS BROCCARD</header>
<page-num>216</page-num>

...against the said Provinces, and they promise every effort if they should arrive there. In recent months, the **Jesuits**, who live in disguise <note>original: "personati"; literally 'masked,' referring to Jesuits living undercover in Protestant territories where their order was banned</note> and celebrate Mass in the said Provinces, wrote to **Claudio Acquaviva** <note>The 5th Superior General of the Society of Jesus, who oversaw the order's global expansion</note>, their Superior General, stating that the number of Papists able to bear arms in the Provinces of **Batavia** <note>Holland</note> and **Frisia** <note>Friesland</note> alone amounts to 30,000 and more. These men were so emboldened that they were prepared at any opportunity to shed their blood for the **Roman Church**. 

Their letters were read in the **Congregation for the Propagation of the Faith** <note>The Vatican department (Propaganda Fide) established to coordinate missionary work and the "re-conversion" of Protestant lands</note>, as they call it, and with such applause that everyone judged that if any fleet were to arrive there, the greatest uprisings could easily be stirred up. They believed the best success could be hoped for the **Catholics** in those Provinces if matters should only once succeed fortunately for **Albert** <note>Archduke Albert VII of Austria</note>. Indeed, the **Estates** <note>The States General, the governing body of the Dutch Republic</note> ought to restrain the insolence of these rebels with greater severity, lest they become worse through excessive indulgence.

41. The German clergy who live along the **Rhine**, such as those of **Mainz** and **Cologne** <note>The Prince-Archbishops of these cities were powerful Electors of the Holy Roman Empire</note>, warned the Roman Pope that throughout **Germany**, confederations are being formed among the **Evangelical Princes**, and frequent meetings are being held. One faction seeks to defend **Calvinism** against **Lutheranism**, while the other side stands for **Lutheranism** against **Calvinism**. At this very time, they are more discordant, incensed, and irritated among themselves than they ever were before against the Papists. They added many other more specific details concerning the factio-

### A Trumpet-Call against the Pope

<page-num>217</page-num>

...also in political actions and regarding other rivalries and hatreds between both Religions <note>Referring to the ongoing conflict between Catholicism and Protestantism</note> toward each other, as well as dissensions among the same [Protestants] along with other feelings of the common people toward their Princes. They intended to start from there, hoping that the **Evangelicals** <note>Protestants</note> would soon clash with one another, and that in this way the **Roman Religion** <note>The Roman Catholic Church</note> could be easily spread. 

The aforementioned confederates, seizing this opportunity, spare neither labor nor expense to nourish and increase such dissensions, contentions, and hatreds among the Evangelicals. What is pernicious and miserably to be deplored is that they have achieved so much by their cunning and our own laziness that they even use the—I know not whether to say simplicity or malice—of **Evangelical Princes and Councilors** to excite and foster these things. 

Many in our time are so fascinated by the deceits of the **Papists** <note>A common, often derogatory, term for Roman Catholics during the Reformation</note> and the arts of certain courtiers that they persuade themselves as a certainty that the Monarchs and Princes of the Papists do not wish ill toward the Gospel; and what is more, that the Pope himself favors the [Protestant] Religion and desires peace with our people. Circumvented by this empty confidence and lulled as if by a deadly lethargy, those who ought to watch over the **Flock of the Lord** <note>The Christian congregation</note> are sleeping a deep sleep.

I beg you, Most Illustrious Princes, as many as you are, who have renounced the **Papal idol-madness** <note>original: "idolomania"; a polemical term used by reformers to describe the Catholic use of icons and the authority of the Pope</note> and favor the Holy Gospel—whether it be under the name of **Luther** or **Calvin** <note>Referring to the two main branches of the Reformation: Lutheranism and Reformed/Calvinist Christianity</note>—that you lay aside your rivalries...

<summary>This page warns Protestant leaders that Catholic forces are exploiting internal divisions between Lutherans and Calvinists to weaken the Reformation. The author critiques the "lethargy" of Protestant princes who believe the Pope or Catholic monarchs desire a genuine peace.</summary>

<keywords>Reformation, Protestantism, Catholicism, Religious Warfare, Martin Luther, John Calvin, Papacy, Jesuits, Diplomacy</keywords>

<meta>This page concludes Francesco Broccardo’s polemic, continuing the urgent warning from page 217 regarding the perceived conspiracies of the Catholic Church against Protestant ("Evangelical") princes.</meta>

### Francesco Broccardo’s Trumpet-Call against the Pope

<page-num>218</page-num>

...[strife] and hatreds, if indeed there are any, you should all come together into one holy and truly sacred **covenant** <note>original: "foedus"; a formal alliance or treaty</note> against your declared and sworn enemies. You must believe that all **Papists** <note>original: "Pontificios"; a term used by reformers to denote those loyal to the Pope</note>, whoever they may be, are your worst enemies and foes. 

If you do not believe my simple account, I beg you to observe their actions and movements. For you will easily discover that whatever I have encompassed in this writing is most true; in this work, I have not considered what was convenient for myself, but rather what would be beneficial for you and for the entire **Evangelical realm** <note>The collective territories and political interests of the Protestant Reformation</note>. 

I have also omitted many specific details so as not to be too long-winded; if I learn that you are making timely use of these warnings (as is fitting), I shall not fail to add those details for the common benefit of the Evangelicals. In the meantime: keep watch, guard yourselves, and give thanks to God.

->**Francesco Broccardo**,<-
->*Secretary to the Pope.*<-

---

->THE END.<-

<summary>In this concluding passage, Francesco Broccardo urges Protestant leaders to set aside internal divisions and form a sacred covenant against the Papacy. He presents himself as a selfless whistleblower, offering his insider knowledge as a former Papal Secretary to protect the Evangelical cause.</summary>

<keywords>Francesco Broccardo, Papacy, Evangelical realm, Protestant Reformation, covenant, religious conflict, Papal Secretary</keywords>
